A set
\Omega\setminus\overline{E}
that shows that the constant in (
2.3
)(ii) is sharp, for instance for
\Omega=(0,1)^{2}
. The set
E
is depicted in gray.

Example 2.7

(Sharpness of constant)
.

The constant in (
2.3
)(ii) is sharp. To this end, consider
\Omega
and
E
in dimension
d=2
as depicted in Figure
3
, and note that the thickening of the set
E
will not disconnect
\Omega\setminus\overline{E}
, see (
2.2
)(i). The set
\Omega\setminus\overline{E}
consists essentially of
m\sim\gamma^{-1/q}
“vertical stripes” depicted with white color in the picture. We define a deformation
y
on
\Omega\setminus\overline{E}
which on each of the stripes bends by an angle of
\sigma:=\gamma^{1/q}
as indicated in (
2.8
) (for
\delta:=3\gamma^{1/q}
) such that between the first and the last stripe a macroscopic rotation is performed. Repeating the argument in (
2.9
), we get
\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}}\dist^{2}(\nabla y,SO(2))\,{\rm d}x\lesssim m(\gamma^{1/q})^{2}(3\gamma^{1/q})^{3}/3\sim\gamma^{4/q}
and on the other hand
\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}}|\nabla y-R|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\geq c
for all
R\in SO(2)
.

2.2.
Proof of Theorem
2.1

This subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
2.1
. We start with a short outline of the proof collecting the main intermediate steps. The core of our proof is the construction of the thickened set
E_{\eta,\gamma}
with the properties in (
2.2
). We formulate this in a separate auxiliary result, and for this purpose we recall the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}
in (
2.1
).

Proposition 2.8

(
Thickening of sets).

Let
d=2,3
,
q\in[d-1,+\infty)
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
, and
\varphi
be a norm on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open and bounded and let
\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\subset\Omega
be an open subset. Then, there exist a constant
C_{0}=C_{0}(\varphi)>0
,
\eta_{0}\in(0,1)
depending only on
{\rm dist}(\partial\Omega,\widetilde{\Omega})
and
\varphi
, and for each
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
there exists
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in(0,1)
, with
c_{\eta}\to 0
as
\eta\to 0
, such that the following holds:

Given
E\in\mathcal{A}_{\rm reg}(\Omega)
, we can find an open set
E_{\eta,\gamma}
such that
E\subset E_{\eta,\gamma}\subset\Omega
,
\partial E_{\eta,\gamma}\cap\Omega
is a union of finitely many regular submanifolds, and

(2.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm(i)}&\ \ \mathrm{dist}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x,E\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\geq c_{\eta}\gamma^{1/q}\quad\text{for all $x\in\big\{y\in\Omega\setminus\overline{E_{\eta,\gamma}}\colon\dist(y,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})<c_{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{1/q}\big\}$}\,,\\
{\rm(ii)}&\ \ \mathcal{L}^{d}(E_{\eta,\gamma}\setminus E)\leq\eta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{1/q}\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(E),\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\quad\quad\dist_{\mathcal{H}}(E,E_{\eta,\gamma})\leq\eta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{1/q}\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\\
{\rm(iii)}&\ \ \int_{\partial E_{\eta,\gamma}\cap\Omega}\varphi(\nu_{E_{\eta,\gamma}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\leq(1+C_{0}\eta\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\,\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(E)\,.\end{split}

We defer the proof to Subsection
2.3
below. Note that (
2.10
)(ii),(iii) are exactly the properties stated in the main result, see (
2.2
). The additional property (
2.10
)(i) is essential for the proof of (
2.3
) as it allows to cover

(2.11)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}_{\eta,\gamma}^{E}:=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\overline{E_{\eta,\gamma}}

with cubes which are all contained in
\Omega\setminus\overline{E}
. More precisely, for
r>0
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
open and bounded, recalling the definition in (
1.6
), we define the
r
-cubic set
corresponding to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
by

(2.12)

\displaystyle(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{r}:={\rm int}\,\Big(\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}{Q}_{r}\Big)\,,

where
\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}):=\{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}\colon\,Q_{r}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\}
.
We define

(2.13)

\displaystyle r_{\eta,\gamma}:=\frac{c_{\eta}\,\gamma^{1/q}}{2\sqrt{d}}\,,

where
c_{\eta}
is the constant of Proposition
2.8
. Now, by using (
2.10
)(i) and
c_{\eta}\to 0
as
\eta\to 0
, by possibly passing to a smaller constant
\eta_{0}
depending on
{\rm dist}(\partial\Omega,\widetilde{\Omega})
one can check that

(2.14)

\displaystyle Q_{r_{\eta,\gamma}}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{\eta,\gamma}}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}_{\eta,\gamma}^{E}\big)\quad\quad\Rightarrow\quad\quad Q_{2r_{\eta,\gamma}}\subset\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\,.

For general
r
-cubic sets the following rigidity result holds.

Proposition 2.9

(Rigidity on
r
-cubic sets)
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open and bounded, let
r>0
, and suppose that the
r
-cubic set
(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{r}
defined in (
2.12
) is connected. Then, there exists an absolute constant
C>0
independent of
U
and
r
such that for all
y\in H^{1}((\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{r};\mathbb{R}^{d})
there exist
R\in SO(d)
and
b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

(2.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\rm(i)}\quad&\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{(U)^{r}}|\nabla y-R|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\big(\#\mathcal{Q}_{r}(U)\big)^{2}\int_{(U)^{r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\rm(ii)}\quad&\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r^{-2}\int_{(U)^{r}}|y(x)-(R\,x+b)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\big(\#\mathcal{Q}_{r}(U)\big)^{4}\int_{(U)^{r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.\end{split}

Additionally, if there exists
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
with
\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap\{\nabla y={\rm Id}\})\geq cr^{d}
for some absolute constant
c\in(0,1)
, then (
2.15
) holds for
R={\rm Id}
, for a constant
C>0
depending on
c
.

The result is a direct consequence of the rigidity estimate (
1.1
) proved by
Friesecke, James, and Müller

[
50
]
, applied on a cube, along with estimating the variation of the rotations on different cubes. Although the latter argument is well-known and has been performed, e.g., in
[
50
, Section 4]
, we include a short proof in Appendix
A.3
for convenience of the reader.

Observe that typically one has
\#\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\sim\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{L}^{d}(U)r^{-d}
, which along with (
2.13
) explains the scaling in (
2.3
)(ii). The proof of (
2.3
)(i) instead will rely on Proposition
2.9
along with the linearization formula
[
50
, (3.20)]
,

(2.16)

\displaystyle|{\rm sym}(R^{T}F-{\rm Id})|={\rm dist}(F,SO(d))+{\rm O}(|F-R|^{2})

for
F\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}SO(d)
. In fact, the latter shows that it suffices to have a good bound on
\int|\nabla y-R|^{4}\,{\rm d}x
in order to control the symmetrized gradient in
L^{2}
. We are now ready to give the proof of Theorem
2.1
.

Proof of Theorem
2.1
.

Let
q\in[d-1,+\infty)
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\subset\Omega
be an open subset, and let
\varphi
be a norm on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Without restriction we can assume that
\widetilde{\Omega}
is smooth. We let
\eta_{0}
as in Proposition
2.8
and
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
. We can assume that for
c_{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
given in Proposition
2.8
also (
2.14
) holds, where
r_{\eta,\gamma}
is defined as in (
2.13
). From now on, we write
r
in place of
r_{\eta,\gamma}
for notational simplicity.

We let
E_{\eta,\gamma}
be the set obtained from Proposition
2.8
. In particular, (
2.2
) holds by (
2.10
)(ii),(iii). Let
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}_{\eta,\gamma}^{E}
be the set in (
2.11
), and denote by
(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})_{j}
the connected components of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}_{\eta,\gamma}^{E}
. Note that these are finitely many due to the regularity of
E_{\eta,\gamma}
and
\widetilde{\Omega}
. The main part of the proof now consists in deriving (
2.3
). To this end, similarly to the proof in
[
50
]
, a crucial step is to reduce the problem to harmonic mappings, see Steps 1–2 below. In Steps 3–4 we then provide the rigidity estimate (
2.3
)(i),(ii), and briefly indicate the Poincaré-type estimate (
2.3
)(iii) in Step 5. In the following,
C>0
denotes a generic constant only depending
\Omega
, which may change from line to line. Without restriction, we suppose that the sets

(2.17)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}:={\rm int}\Big(\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}_{j}^{\eta,\gamma})}\overline{Q_{2r}}\Big)\quad\text{ are pairwise disjoint}.

Indeed, whenever
\hat{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{i}\cap\hat{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}\neq\emptyset
, one can replace
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{i}
and
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}
in the reasoning below by
\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{i}\cup\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}
and can derive (
2.3
) for a single rotation on
\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{i}\cup\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}
.

Step 1
.(Reduction to Lipschitz mappings on cubes). For every cube
Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}_{\eta,\gamma}^{E})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
we have
Q_{2r}\subset\Omega\setminus\overline{E}
by (
2.14
). By a variant of
[
38
, Theorem 6.15]
, see also
[
50
, Proposition A.1]
, we let
y_{Q}\in W^{1,\infty}(Q_{2r};\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a Lipschitz truncation obtained from
y
satisfying

(2.18)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm(i)}&\ \ \|\nabla y_{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2r})}\leq C\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\\
{\rm(ii)}&\ \ \int_{Q_{2r}}|\nabla y-\nabla y_{Q}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int_{Q_{2r}\cap\{|\nabla y|>2\sqrt{d}\}}|\nabla y|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.\end{split}

Here, with a slight abuse of notation, we write
y_{Q}
instead of
y_{Q_{r}}
. We now claim that it suffices to prove that there exist
(R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})_{j}\subset SO(d)
such that

(2.19)

\displaystyle\hskip-1.99997pt\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{r}}\big|{\rm sym}\big((R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})^{T}\nabla y_{Q}-{\rm Id}\big)\big|^{2}\,\leq C(1+r^{-5d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,,

where here and in the following we use the shorthand notation
\varepsilon:=\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x
, and

(2.20)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{r}}\big|(R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})^{T}\nabla y_{Q}-{\rm Id}\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq Cr^{-2d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Indeed, let us note that

(2.21)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{2r}\cap\{|\nabla y|>2\sqrt{d}\}}|\nabla y|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int_{Q_{2r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

since
|F|\leq 2\mathrm{dist}(F,SO(d))
for all
F\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
with
|F|>2\sqrt{d}
.
This along with (
1.7
), (
2.14
), (
2.17
), and (
2.18
)(ii) shows that

(2.22)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{Q_{2r}}|\nabla y-\nabla y_{Q}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq C\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Then, (
2.3
)(i),(ii) for a constant
C_{\eta}=C_{\eta}(\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega,\widetilde{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})>0
and
C_{0}>0
(depending on
\Omega
) clearly follows from (
2.19
)–(
2.20
), (
2.22
), the triangle inequality, the definition of
r=r_{\eta,\gamma}
in (
2.13
), and the definition of
\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})
below (
2.12
). Therefore, it suffices to prove (
2.19
)–(
2.20
).

Step 2
.(Reduction to harmonic mappings)
For every
Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}_{\eta,\gamma}^{E})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we consider
y_{Q}=w_{Q}+z_{Q}
, where, in the sense of distributions,

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta w_{Q}=0&\text{on }Q_{2r},\\
w_{Q}=y_{Q}&\text{on }\partial Q_{2r},\end{cases}\quad\text{ and }\quad\begin{cases}\Delta z_{Q}=\mathrm{div}\left(\nabla y_{Q}-\mathrm{cof}\nabla y_{Q}\right)&\text{on }Q_{2r},\\
z_{Q}=0&\text{on }\partial Q_{2r}.\end{cases}

It holds that

(2.23)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{2r}}|\nabla z_{Q}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\int_{Q_{2r}}|\mathrm{cof}\nabla y_{Q}-\nabla y_{Q}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\int_{Q_{2r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y_{Q},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

In fact, this follows from the arguments in
[
50
, Proof of Theorem 3.1, Step 1]
, in particular using that
|\mathrm{cof}F-F|\leq c\,{\rm dist}(F,SO(d))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for all
F\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
with
|F|\leq C
for some
c=c(C)>0
, where here
C
denotes the constant of (
2.18
)(i). In view of (
2.18
)(ii) and (
2.21
), (
2.23
) implies

(2.24)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{2r}}|\nabla y_{Q}-\nabla w_{Q}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int_{Q_{2r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y_{Q},SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int_{Q_{2r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

This along with (
1.7
), (
2.14
), and (
2.17
) shows that, in order to establish (
2.19
)–(
2.20
), it suffices to show that there exist
(R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})_{j}\subset SO(d)
such that

(2.25)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{r}}\big|{\rm sym}\big((R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})^{T}\nabla w_{Q}-{\rm Id}\big)\big|^{2}\leq C(1+r^{-5d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))

and

(2.26)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{r}}\big|(R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})^{T}\nabla w_{Q}-{\rm Id}\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq Cr^{-2d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Step 3
.(Local (
L^{2}
-
L^{\infty}
)-estimate for harmonic mappings) In this step we show that for each
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}_{j}^{\eta,\gamma}
there exists
R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}\in SO(d)
such that

(2.27)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{2r}}|\nabla w_{Q}-R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq Cr^{-2d}\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

and for each
Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})
it holds that

(2.28)

\displaystyle\|\nabla w_{Q}-R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{r})}\leq Cr^{-3d/2}\left(\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Cr^{-3d/2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\,,

where we recall the notation for
\varepsilon
below (
2.19
). To see this, we apply Proposition
2.9
for
2r/3
in place of
r
on the function
y
and on the sets
\hat{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}
introduced in (
2.17
) in place of
U
. In view of the fact that
\hat{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}=(\hat{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})^{2r/3}
, we find
(R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})_{j}\subset SO(d)
such that

(2.29)

\displaystyle\int_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}}|\nabla y-R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq Cr^{-2d}\int_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

where we used that
\#\mathcal{Q}_{2r/3}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega)(2r/3)^{-d}
, i.e.,
C
in (
2.29
) also depends on
\Omega
. By (
1.7
) this yields

(2.30)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{2r}}\big|(R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})^{T}\nabla y-{\rm Id}\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq Cr^{-2d}\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

where as before we employed also (
2.14
) and (
2.17
). In view of (
1.7
), (
2.14
), (
2.17
), (
2.22
), (
2.24
), and the triangle inequality we get

(2.31)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{2r}}|\nabla w_{Q}-\nabla y|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Consequently, by (
2.30
) we finally obtain (
2.27
).

We now address (
2.28
).
For every
j
and every
Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})
, due to (
2.27
), the fact that
w_{Q}
is a harmonic mapping on
Q_{2r}
and
Q_{2r}\subset\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, as a consequence of the mean value property and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

(2.32)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|\nabla w_{Q}-R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{r})}\leq\frac{C}{r^{d/2}}\left(\int_{Q_{2r}}|\nabla w_{Q}-R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C}{r^{3d/2}}\left(\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

This yields (
2.28
), and Step 3 of the proof is concluded.

Step 4
.(Global estimates) In this step we finally prove (
2.3
)(i),(ii). In view of Step 2, it suffices to check (
2.25
)–(
2.26
). First, (
2.26
) follows directly from
(
2.27
). By the linearization formula (
2.16
), (
2.28
), (
2.31
), and Young’s inequality we have

(2.33)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum\nolimits_{j}&\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{r}}\big|{\rm sym}\big((R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})^{T}\nabla w_{Q}-{\rm Id}\big)\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq C\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\Big(\int_{Q_{r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla w_{Q},SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{Q_{r}}|\nabla w_{Q}-R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}|^{4}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\\
&\leq C\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x+Cr^{-3d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{r}}|\nabla w_{Q}-R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.\end{split}

Then, by using (
2.27
) we get

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{r}}\big|{\rm sym}\big((R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})^{T}\nabla w_{Q}-{\rm Id}\big)\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C(1+r^{-5d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

This yields (
2.25
) and concludes the proof of (
2.3
)(i),(ii).

Step 5
.(Poincaré estimate) We briefly indicate how to derive (
2.3
)(iii). By applying (
2.15
)
\rm{(ii)}
of Proposition
2.9
for
2r/3
in place of
r
on the function
y(x)-R_{j}^{\eta,\gamma}x
and on
\hat{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}
in place of
U
, using again that
\#\mathcal{Q}_{2r/3}(\hat{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega)(2r/3)^{-d}
, we also find
(b^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})_{j}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

(2.34)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\widetilde{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})}\int_{Q_{2r}}\big|y(x)-(R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}\,x+b^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq Cr^{2-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}4\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}\int_{\Omega\setminus\overline{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}.

Recalling the definition of
r=r_{\eta,\gamma}
in (
2.13
), and the definition of
\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})
below (
2.12
) we conclude (
2.3
)(iii).
∎

Remark 2.10
.

A closer inspection of the proof shows that Theorem
2.1
can be
localized
, in the sense that in the rigidity estimates (
2.3
) the sets
\Omega,\widetilde{\Omega}
can be replaced by open sets
U,\widetilde{U}
respectively, with
\widetilde{U}\subset\subset U\subset\Omega
,
\widetilde{U}\subset\widetilde{\Omega}
, and
{\rm dist}(\partial U,\widetilde{U})\geq{\rm dist}(\partial\Omega,\widetilde{\Omega})
, where the connected components of
\widetilde{U}\setminus\overline{E}_{\eta,\gamma}
would then be denoted by
(\widetilde{U}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j})_{j}
. In that case, the constant
C_{\eta}
does not depend on
\Omega
,
\widetilde{\Omega}
, but only on
\eta
and on
(\mathcal{L}^{3}(U))^{2}
.

Indeed, the above arguments essentially rely on (
2.14
) and the estimates on cubic sets given in Proposition
2.9
. The estimate
{\rm dist}(\partial U,\widetilde{U})\geq{\rm dist}(\partial\Omega,\widetilde{\Omega})
guarantees (
2.14
) for
U
,
\widetilde{U}
. The fact that the constant
C>0
appearing in (
2.29
) depends quadratically on
\mathcal{L}^{3}(U)
follows by the comment just below it, while by scaling invariance,
C>0
appearing in (
2.32
) can be chosen to be an absolute constant. Therefore, the estimates in (
2.33
) yield this precise dependence.

We close this subsection with the short proof of Corollary
2.2
.

Proof of Corollary
2.2
.

A careful inspection of the previous proof shows that we only need to check that, whenever
\mathcal{L}^{d}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}\cap U_{D})>0
holds, then in (
2.29
) we can choose
R^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}={\rm Id}
. To this end, when
\mathcal{L}^{d}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}\cap U_{D})>0
, we find
Q_{r}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{j}^{\eta,\gamma})
such that
Q_{r}\cap U_{D}\neq\emptyset
. Then, we can select
Q^{\prime}_{2r/3}\in\mathcal{Q}_{2r/3}(\hat{\Omega}_{j}^{\eta,\gamma})
,
Q_{2r/3}^{\prime}\subset Q_{2r}\subset\hat{\Omega}^{\eta,\gamma}_{j}
, see (
2.17
), such that by (
2.13
), the fact that
\gamma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in(0,1)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and by the fact that
U_{D}
has Lipschitz boundary we get
\mathcal{L}^{d}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{Q^{\prime}_{2r/3}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap U_{D})\geq cr^{d}
for a small absolute constant
c\in(0,1)
, provided that
c_{\eta}
is sufficiently small also depending on
U_{D}
. Then the desired property follows from the additional statement in Proposition
2.9
and the fact that
y={\rm id}
on
U_{D}
. In this context, note that the constant
C_{\eta}
in (
2.3
) depends on
c_{\eta}
and therefore
C_{\eta}
also depends on
U_{D}
.
∎

2.3.
Thickening of sets

In this subsection we prove Proposition
2.8
. Without restriction we will assume from now on that
\varphi_{\rm min}:=\min_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\varphi=1
. Indeed, we can simply perform the proof for
\varphi_{\rm min}^{-1}\varphi
in place of
\varphi
and
\varphi_{\rm min}^{-1}\gamma
in place of
\gamma
to see that (
2.10
)(iii) holds. The proof essentially relies on a local construction to thicken the set
E
in a suitable way. To formulate the local statement, we introduce some further notation. Given
\rho>0
and a cube
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}
, see (
1.6
), we denote the set of
neighboring cubes
by

(2.35)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(Q_{\rho}):=\big\{Q_{\rho}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}\colon\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial Q_{\rho}\cap\partial Q^{\prime}_{\rho})>0\big\}\,.

Note that
\#\mathcal{N}(Q_{\rho})=2d
. We also recall the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}
in (
2.1
). Moreover, for notational convenience, we denote the anisotropic perimeter by

(2.36)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\Gamma):=\int_{\Gamma}\varphi(\nu_{\Gamma})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

for a norm
\varphi
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and for a
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(d-1)
-rectifiable set
\Gamma
, where
\nu_{\Gamma}
denotes a measure-theoretical unit normal to
\Gamma
. Note that the integral is invariant under changing the orientation of
\nu_{\Gamma}
as
\varphi
is a norm. The proof of Proposition
2.8
will make use of the following lemma, whose proof will be given later in Subsection
2.5
.

Lemma 2.11

(Local thickening of sets)
.

Let
d=2,3
,
q\in[d-1,+\infty)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
, and
\varphi
be a norm on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open and bounded,
\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\subset\Omega
be an open subset, and let
\Lambda>0
. Then, there exist constants
C=C(\varphi,\Lambda)>0
and
\eta_{0}=\eta_{0}(\Lambda)\in(0,1)
such that for all
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
the following holds:

For every
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho\leq\eta^{7}\gamma^{1/q}
and for each
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{12\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\Omega
, and

(2.37)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(E\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{8\rho})\leq\Lambda\rho^{d-1},\ \ \mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(E\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\prime}_{8\rho})\leq\Lambda\rho^{d-1}\ \ \text{$\forall\ Q_{\rho}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(Q_{\rho})$}\,,

we can find pairwise disjoint sets
(\Gamma_{i})_{i=1}^{I}
in
\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho}
with
I\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, corresponding closed sets
(T_{i})_{i=1}^{I}\subset Q_{8\rho}
, with
\partial T_{i}
being a union of finitely many regular submanifolds and a decomposition
\{1,\ldots,I\}=\mathcal{I}_{\rm good}\cup\bigcup_{Q^{\prime}_{\rho}\in\mathcal{N}(Q_{\rho})}\mathcal{I}_{\rm bad}(Q^{\prime}_{\rho})
such that

(2.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm(i)}&\ \ \mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\big(\partial T_{i}\setminus\overline{E}\big)\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\Gamma_{i}\cap Q_{\rho})+C\eta\rho^{d-1}\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\forall\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i\in\mathcal{I}_{\rm good},\\[1.0pt]
{\rm(ii)}&\ \ \mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\big(\partial T_{i}\setminus\overline{E}\big)\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\big(\Gamma_{i}\cap(Q_{\rho}\cup Q_{\rho}^{\prime})\big)+C\eta\rho^{d-1}\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\forall\ Q_{\rho}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(Q_{\rho}),\,\forall\,i\in\mathcal{I}_{\rm bad}(Q^{\prime}_{\rho}),\end{split}

and

(2.39)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\rm dist}\Big(\partial E\cap Q_{\rho},\Big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E\cup\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bigcup\nolimits_{i=1}^{I}T_{i}\Big)^{c}\Big)\geq\eta\rho\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Moreover, fixing
Q^{\prime}_{\rho}\in\mathcal{N}(Q_{\rho})
, introducing the notation
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\rm bad}(Q_{\rho})
as above with respect to the cube
Q^{\prime}_{\rho}
, and letting
\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}
and
T_{i}^{\prime}
be the corresponding sets, we have

(2.40)

\displaystyle i\in\mathcal{I}_{\rm bad}(Q_{\rho}^{\prime})\quad\Rightarrow\quad\text{$\exists$ $j\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\rm bad}(Q_{\rho})$ such that $(\Gamma_{i}\triangle\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})\cap(Q_{\rho}\cup Q_{\rho}^{\prime})=\emptyset$ and $T_{i}=T_{j}^{\prime}$.}

Properties (
2.38
)(i) and (
2.39
) are the fundamental points of the lemma: essentially, in the proof we show that the connected components of
\partial E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
can be covered with thin polyhedra, leading to the definition of the sets
(T_{i})_{i}
. The case (
2.38
)(ii) is only of technical nature, as additional care is needed if a component of
\partial E\cap Q_{\rho}
is close to a neighboring cube, see Figure
4
.

The construction of
E_{\eta,\gamma}
in Proposition
2.8
will rely on suitably modifying
E
by applying Lemma
2.11
on cubes intersecting
\partial E
. To this end, we consider the tessellation of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with the family of cubes
\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}
for
\rho=\eta^{7}\gamma^{1/q}
, so that Lemma
2.11
is applicable. In this context, it is important to control the number of
boundary cubes
, given by

(2.41)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big\{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}\colon\,\partial E\cap Q_{\rho}\neq\emptyset,\,\overline{Q_{12\rho}}\subset\Omega\big\}\,.\ \color[rgb]{0,0,0}

This will be achieved by the following lemma, whose proof will be given in the next subsection.

Lemma 2.12

(Small area implies large curvature)
.

Let
d=2,3
,
\Lambda\color[rgb]{0,0,0}>0
,
q\in[d-1,+\infty)
, and
\gamma\in(0,1)
. Then, there exists an absolute constant
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{0}>0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and a constant
c_{\Lambda}>0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
only depending on
\Lambda
such that for all
0<\rho\leq c_{\Lambda}\gamma^{1/q}
,
E\in\mathcal{A}_{\rm reg}(\Omega)
, and
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{8\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\Omega
and
\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset
, the following implication holds true:

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<c_{0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{d-1}\implies\gamma\int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}>\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Lambda\rho^{d-1}\,.

Indeed, the implication shows that whenever the surface
\partial E
inside a cube has small but nonzero area, then necessarily the curvature contribution is high. This will allow us to control the number of boundary cubes, see particularly (
2.52
) and (
2.3
) in the proof below. The result is a consequence of
[
88
, Corollary 1.3]
and we present its proof in Subsection
2.4
below. Let us mention that the analog of Lemma
2.12
is the main obstacle to generalize our result to higher dimensions, see Remark
2.22
for more details in this direction.

Example 2.13
.

The statement of Lemma
2.12
is false for
q<d-1
. In fact, let
E=B_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\subset Q_{8\rho}
be a ball of radius
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}>0
small. Then, clearly
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho})<c_{0}\rho^{d-1}
for
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
small enough. On the other hand,
\int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
coincides up to a constant with
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma{}^{d-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma{}^{-q}
.

We are now in the position to give the proof of Proposition
2.8
.

Proof of Proposition
2.8
.

Recall that without restriction we have assumed that
\min_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\varphi\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1
. In the following proof we will write
\varphi_{\max}=\max_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\varphi
for brevity. First of all, we define the constant
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Lambda:=2d\,12^{d-1}15^{d}\varphi_{\max}
, whose role will become clear in (
2.55
) below. For this
\Lambda
, we apply Lemma
2.11
to obtain
\eta_{0}
, and from now on we fix
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
. We consider the tessellation of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with the collection of cubes
\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}
, where

(2.42)

\displaystyle\rho:=\eta^{7}\gamma^{1/q}

is chosen in such a way that Lemma
2.11
is applicable. Here, without restriction, up to passing to a smaller constant
\eta_{0}
, we can assume that
\eta_{0}\leq c^{1/7}_{\Lambda}
, and therefore also Lemma
2.12
is applicable. Moreover, we can further choose
\eta_{0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}>0
also depending on
\Omega,\widetilde{\Omega}
such that for all
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
we have
20\sqrt{d}\rho\leq\eta\dist(\widetilde{\Omega},\partial\Omega)
. Then, with a standard layering argument and recalling (
1.5
), we can find an open set
\Omega^{\prime}
with
\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\subset\Omega^{\prime}\subset\subset\Omega
, and

(2.43)

\displaystyle(\partial\Omega^{\prime})_{1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}5\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqrt{d}\rho}\subset\Omega\setminus\widetilde{\Omega},

such that for a constant
C>0
only depending on
\varphi
it holds that

(2.44)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\big(\partial E\cap(\partial\Omega^{\prime})_{3\sqrt{d}\rho}\big)\leq C\rho(\dist(\widetilde{\Omega},\partial\Omega))^{-1}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial E\cap\Omega)\leq C\eta\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial E\cap\Omega)\,.

Note that the choice of
\eta_{0}
ensures that
\eta_{0}
depends only on
{\rm dist}(\partial\Omega,\widetilde{\Omega})
and
\varphi
.

Step 1
.(Boundary cubes)
We define the collection of
boundary cubes
by

(2.45)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}:=\big\{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}\colon\,\overline{Q_{12\rho}}\subset\Omega\text{\ \ \color[rgb]{0,0,0}and: \color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ }\partial E\cap Q_{\rho}\neq\emptyset\text{\ \ or \ }\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E;Q_{8\rho})>\Lambda\rho^{d-1}\,\big\}\,.

(For technical reasons, the definition slightly differs from (
2.41
) mentioned above.) We decompose
\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}
as follows: first, we let
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm acc}_{\rho}
be the collection of the cubes
Q_{\rho}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
satisfying

(2.46)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{8\rho})>\Lambda\rho^{d-1}\,.

This definition collects the cubes whose 8-times enlargement
accumulates
a lot of surface energy. We further let
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm neigh}_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}\setminus\mathcal{Q}^{\rm acc}_{\rho}
be the collection of cubes
Q_{\rho}
in a
neighborhood
of
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm acc}_{\rho}
, i.e.,

(2.47)

\displaystyle\text{there exists}\quad Q^{\prime}_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}\quad\text{such that}\quad Q_{\rho}\subset Q^{\prime}_{12\rho}\,.

Eventually, we set
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm flat}_{\rho}:=\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}\setminus(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm acc}_{\rho}\cup\mathcal{Q}^{\rm neigh}_{\rho})
. As we will see in the statement of Lemmata
2.15
–
2.16
below, the latter collection corresponds to the cubes where the surface
\partial E
is approximately
flat
. For later purposes, we observe that by applying Lemma
2.12
we find that

(2.48)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho})\geq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{d-1}\ \text{\ and \ }\ \mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{8\rho})\leq\Lambda\rho^{d-1}\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\forall\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm neigh}_{\rho}\cup\mathcal{Q}^{\rm flat}_{\rho}\,.

The set
E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}
be will defined by

(2.49)

\displaystyle E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\mathrm{int}\big(E\cup\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}}E_{\eta,\gamma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(Q_{\rho})\big)\,,

where the definition of the sets
E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}(Q_{\rho})
for
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}
is given in Step 2 of the proof. In Step 3 we address (
2.10
)(i),(ii), and eventually Step 4 is devoted to the proof of (
2.10
)(iii).

Step 2
.(Definition of the sets
E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho})
). We address the three cases
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm acc}_{\rho}
,
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm neigh}_{\rho}
, and
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm flat}_{\rho}
separately.

(a) First, if
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm acc}_{\rho}
, we set
E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(Q_{\rho}):=\overline{Q_{12\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.

(b) If
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm neigh}_{\rho}
, we set
E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(Q_{\rho}):=\emptyset
.

(c) Finally, we address the case of
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm flat}_{\rho}
. If
Q_{\rho}\cap\Omega^{\prime}=\emptyset
, we let
E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(Q_{\rho}):=\emptyset
. Otherwise, by (
2.43
) we have
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{14\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\Omega
and, in view of (
2.45
) and (
2.47
), for every cube
Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}(Q_{\rho})
we have that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{surf}}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(E;Q^{\prime}_{8\rho})\leq\Lambda\rho^{d-1}.
This along with (
2.48
) allows to apply Lemma
2.11
for
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}
. We obtain finitely many corresponding pairwise disjoint sets
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Gamma_{i}^{Q})_{i=1}^{I}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
in
\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho}
and closed sets
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(T_{i}^{Q})_{i=1}^{I}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, with
T_{i}^{Q}\subset Q_{8\rho}
and
\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T^{Q}_{i}
being a union of finitely many regular submanifolds, such that (
2.38
)–(
2.40
) hold. In this case, we define

(2.50)

\displaystyle E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho})=\bigcup\nolimits_{i}T^{Q}_{i}\,.

By definition it is clear that
E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}\subset\Omega
and that
\partial E_{\eta,\gamma}\cap\Omega
is a union of finitely many regular submanifolds. We now confirm (
2.10
).

Step 3
.(Proof of (
2.10
)(i),(ii)) We start with the proof of (
2.10
)(i). To this end, it suffices to check that

(2.51)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\dist\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\Omega\setminus\overline{E_{\eta,\gamma}}\big)\geq\eta\rho\quad\text{for all $y\in\partial E\cap\Omega^{\prime}$}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Indeed, let us assume for a moment that we have (
2.51
), and let us set
c_{\eta}:=\eta^{8}
. Consider an arbitrary
x\in\Omega\setminus\overline{E_{\eta,\gamma}}
with
\mathrm{dist}(x,\widetilde{\Omega})<\eta\rho=c_{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{1/q}
, where the last equality follows from the choice of
\rho
in (
2.42
). Since
E\subset E_{\eta,\gamma}
we have that
\mathrm{dist}(x,E)=\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial E)
. In view of (
2.51
) it remains to check that for every
y\in\partial E\setminus\Omega^{\prime}
we have that
|y-x|\geq\eta\rho
. This is trivial by the fact that
\mathrm{dist}(x,\widetilde{\Omega})<\eta\rho
and (
2.43
).

To verify (
2.51
), we first observe that each
y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\partial E\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is contained in some cube of
\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\partial}
, see (
2.43
) and (
2.45
). Therefore, let
y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in Q_{\rho}
for some
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}
with
Q_{\rho}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
. If
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}
, then
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\dist(y,\Omega\setminus E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho}))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\geq 11\rho/2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and (
2.51
) follows in view of (
2.49
). If
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm neigh}
, by (
2.47
) we find some
Q^{\prime}_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}
such that
Q_{\rho}\subset\overline{Q^{\prime}_{12\rho}}=E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho}^{\prime})
. As
\dist(\partial Q_{\rho},\partial Q_{12\rho}^{\prime})\geq\rho/2
by the definition of
\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}
, we get
\dist(y,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega\setminus E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q^{\prime}_{\rho}))\geq\rho/2
, and as before, as long as
0<\eta\leq\eta_{0}\leq 1/2
, (
2.51
) follows from (
2.49
). Eventually, we suppose that
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}
. Then by (
2.39
) along with (
2.50
) we get
\dist(y,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega\setminus E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho}))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\geq\eta\rho
and we conclude as before.

We now show (
2.10
)(ii). The estimate
\dist_{\mathcal{H}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E,E_{\eta,\gamma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq\eta\gamma^{1/q}
follows immediately from (
2.49
) and the fact that each
E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho})
,
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\partial}
, is contained in
\overline{Q_{12\rho}}
, thus having diameter controlled by
12\sqrt{d}\rho\leq\eta\gamma^{1/q}
, for
\eta_{0}
sufficiently small, see the definitions in Step 2 and (
2.42
). In a similar fashion, as
E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho})\subset\overline{Q_{12\rho}}
for all
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}
, and
E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho})=\emptyset
for
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm neigh}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(E_{\eta,\gamma}\setminus E\big)\leq\sum\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho}))\leq(12\rho)^{d}\#\big(\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}\big)\,.

In view of (
2.46
) and (
2.48
) we have
\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E;Q_{8\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\geq\min\{\Lambda,c_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\}\rho^{d-1}
for all
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}
, where we used the fact that we assumed
\varphi_{\rm min}=1
. Now, by (
1.7
) we conclude

(2.52)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(E_{\eta,\gamma}\setminus E\big)\leq C\rho\,\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(E)

for
C>0
depending on
\varphi
. In view of (
2.42
), for
\eta_{0}
sufficiently small this concludes the proof of (
2.10
)(ii).

Step 4
.(Proof of (
2.10
)(iii)) First, the construction of
E_{\eta,\gamma}
in (
2.49
) and (
2.10
)(i) imply that

\partial E_{\eta,\gamma}\cap\Omega\subset\bigcup_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}}(\partial(E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho}))\setminus\overline{E})\cup\partial^{\rm rest}E,\ \ \text{where}\ \ \partial^{\rm rest}E:=(\partial E\cap\Omega)\setminus\bigcup_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}}E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho}).

Hence, as
E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho})=\emptyset
for
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm neigh}
, recalling the notation in (
2.36
), we find

(2.53)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}\cap\Omega)

\displaystyle\leq\hskip-10.81218pt\sum_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm acc}_{\rho}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\big(\partial(E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(Q_{\rho}))\setminus\overline{E}\big)+\hskip-10.81218pt\sum_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm flat}_{\rho}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\big(\partial(E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(Q_{\rho}))\setminus\overline{E}\big)+\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial^{\rm rest}E)\,.

We now estimate the terms on the right hand side of (
2.53
) separately. Let
\mathcal{Q}^{{\rm flat}}_{\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}
be the subset of cubes in
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm flat}_{\rho}
intersecting
\Omega^{\prime}
. First, by construction, in particular by the fact that
\partial E\cap Q_{\rho}\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial E\cap E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho})
for
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm flat}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}
(recall (
2.39
) and the construction in Step 2), we have

(2.54)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial^{\rm rest}E)\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\Big((\partial E\cap\Omega)\setminus\Big(\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{12\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cup\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{{\rm flat}}_{\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}}Q_{\rho}\Big)\Big)\,.

We continue with the first term. Since
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial(E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho})))\leq\varphi_{\max}\,2d(12\rho)^{d-1}
for
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}
, in view of (
2.46
), we calculate

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial(E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho})))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varphi_{\max}\,2d(12\rho)^{d-1}}{\Lambda\rho^{d-1}}\sum\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}}\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{8\rho})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varphi_{\max}\,2d12^{d-1}15^{d}}{\Lambda}\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}\Big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}}Q_{8\rho}\Big)\,,

where in the second step we used that each point in
\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}}Q_{8\rho}
is contained in at most
15^{d}
different cubes
Q_{8\rho}
, see (
1.7
). By the definition of
\Lambda=\varphi_{\max}\,2d\,12^{d-1}15^{d}
at the beginning of the proof, this exactly gives

(2.55)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}(Q_{\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\leq\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}\Big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}}Q_{8\rho}\Big)\,.

Finally, for the second term on the right-hand side of (
2.53
) we will prove that

(2.56)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E_{\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(Q_{\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\overline{E})\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\Big(\partial E\cap\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}^{{\rm flat}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}Q_{3\rho}\Big)+C_{0}\eta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial E\cap\Omega)\,,

for
C_{0}>0
depending only on
\varphi
. To this end, we enumerate the cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}^{{\rm flat}}
by
\{Q^{1}_{\rho},\ldots,Q^{N}_{\rho}\}
, and for each
Q^{n}_{\rho}
,
n=1,\ldots,N
, we denote by
(\Gamma^{n}_{i})_{i=1}^{I_{n}}
the pairwise disjoint sets in
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q_{3\rho}^{n}
and by
(T^{n}_{i})_{i=1}^{I_{n}}
the sets obtained by Lemma
2.11
. Accordingly, we denote the set of indices by
\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\rm good}
and
\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\rm bad}
. We let
\mathcal{J}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,I_{1}\}
, and given
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{J}_{1},\ldots,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{J}_{n-1}
for
n\in\{2,\ldots,N\}
, we define
\mathcal{J}_{n}
as the subset of
\{1,\ldots,I_{n}\}
which does not contain the indices
\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\rm bad}(Q_{\rho}^{\prime})
for
Q^{\prime}_{\rho}\in\mathcal{N}(Q_{\rho}^{n})\cap\{Q^{1}_{\rho},\ldots,Q^{n-1}_{\rho}\}
, i.e., the indices related to parts of
\partial E
which have been covered already by the procedure, related to one of the previous cubes
\{Q^{1}_{\rho},\ldots,Q^{n-1}_{\rho}\}
. Thus, as a consequence of (
2.38
) and (
2.40
), for each
n\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and each
i\in\mathcal{J}_{n}
we find sets
\Psi^{n}_{i}\subset\partial E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q^{n}_{3\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that
(\Psi^{n}_{i})_{n,i}
are pairwise disjoint and

(2.57)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial T^{n}_{i}\setminus\overline{E})\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\Psi^{n}_{i})+C\eta\rho^{d-1}\,.

Indeed, if
i\in\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\rm good}
, one takes
\Psi^{n}_{i}=\Gamma^{n}_{i}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{n}_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. If
i\in\mathcal{I}^{n}_{\rm bad}(Q_{\rho}^{\prime})
, we set
\Psi^{n}_{i}=\Gamma^{n}_{i}\cap(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{n}_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cup Q^{\prime}_{\rho})
. We also note that
\#\mathcal{J}_{n}\leq I_{n}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C=C(\varphi,\Lambda)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, see Lemma
2.11
. The construction along with (
2.57
) shows that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q^{n}_{\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\overline{E})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i\in J_{n}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial T^{n}_{i}\setminus\overline{E})\leq\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i\in J_{n}}(\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\Psi^{n}_{i})+C\eta\rho^{d-1})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\Big(\partial E\cap\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}^{{\rm flat}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{Q_{3\rho}}\Big)+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho^{d-1}\#\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}\,,

where in the last step we used the fact that
(\Psi^{n}_{i})_{n,i}
are pairwise disjoint and their union is contained in
\partial E\cap\bigcup_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}^{{\rm flat}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{Q_{3\rho}}
. This along with (
2.48
) shows that

\displaystyle\hskip-3.8pt\sum_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E_{\eta,\gamma}(Q_{\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\overline{E})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\Big(\partial E\cap\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}^{{\rm flat}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}Q_{3\rho}\Big)+C\eta\sum\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}\big)

(2.58)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}_{\varphi}\Big(\partial E\cap\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}^{{\rm flat}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}Q_{3\rho}\Big)+C\eta\,\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial E\cap\Omega)\,,

where in the last step we again used that each point in
\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}}Q_{8\rho}
is contained in at most
15^{d}
different cubes
Q_{8\rho}
, see (
1.7
), and
Q_{8\rho}\subset\Omega
, see (
2.43
). As
\Lambda
itself is a constant depending only on
\varphi
, we obtain (
2.56
).

We now conclude the proof as follows: note that
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm flat}
implies
Q_{3\rho}\cap Q^{\prime}_{8\rho}=\emptyset
for all
Q^{\prime}_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm acc}_{\rho}
, see (
2.47
). Moreover, we get that

{\Big(\partial E\cap\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}^{{\rm flat}}}Q_{3\rho}\Big)\setminus\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho,\Omega^{\prime}}^{{\rm flat}}}Q_{\rho}\subset\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}^{\rm acc}}\overline{Q_{12\rho}}\cup\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big(\partial E\cap\big(\partial\Omega^{\prime})_{3\sqrt{d}\rho}\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,.}

Then, combining (
2.53
) and (
2.54
)–(
2.56
), and using (
2.43
)–(
2.44
), we obtain (
2.10
)(iii), where
C_{0}>0
indeed only depends on
\varphi
.

∎

We close this subsection with the version for graphs.

Proof of Corollary
2.3
.

Consider
\Omega=\omega\times(-1,M+1)
for some open and bounded
\omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
and
M>0
. Suppose that
E=\{(x^{\prime},x_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\in\Omega\colon\,x^{\prime}\in\omega,x_{d}>h(x^{\prime})\}
for a regular function
h\colon\omega\to[0,M]
.
We start by introducing the set

E_{\eta,\gamma}^{*}=\mathrm{int}\big(E\cup\bigcup\nolimits_{Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\partial}_{\rho}}\overline{Q_{12\rho}}\big)\,.

Clearly, by construction
E_{\eta,\gamma}^{*}\supset E_{\eta,\gamma}
. Moreover, by Lemma
2.12
we find that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{L}^{d}(E_{\eta,\gamma}^{*}\setminus E)\leq C_{0}\rho\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(E),\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\quad\quad\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial E_{\eta,\gamma}^{*}\cap\Omega)\leq C_{0}\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(E)}\,,

for an absolute constant
C_{0}>0
, where we use the definition of
\rho
in (
2.42
). We note that the set
\Omega\setminus E_{\eta,\gamma}^{*}
can be seen as the subgraph of a suitable
BV
-function with
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\partial^{*}E_{\eta,\gamma}^{*}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\partial^{*}E_{\eta,\gamma}^{*})=0
. The desired set
E^{\prime}_{\eta,\gamma}\supset E_{\eta,\gamma}^{*}
is then obtained by approximating the set
\Omega\setminus E_{\eta,\gamma}^{*}
from below with a suitable smooth graph so that (
2.5
) holds true, see
[
29
, Lemma 6.3]
.
∎

2.4.
Small area implies large curvature: Proof of Lemma
2.12

This subsection is devoted to the proof of Lemma
2.12
. We start with a lemma due to
L. Simon
, whose original statement and proof can be found in
[
88
, Corollary 1.3]
. In the next statement, by
\partial\Sigma
we intend the boundary of a regular surface
\Sigma
in the differential-geometric sense.

Lemma 2.14
.

Given
R>0
and
\mu\in(0,1)
, there exist
\alpha_{0}=\alpha_{0}(\mu)>0
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{0}(\mu)>0
such that the following holds: consider a connected, regular, two-dimensional surface
\Sigma
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial\Sigma\cap\overline{B_{R}})=0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma\cap B_{R}}|\bm{A}|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}<\alpha_{0}R,\quad\Sigma\cap\partial B_{R}\neq\emptyset,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\text{and}\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma\cap\partial B_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}\neq\emptyset\,.

Then, we have

\mathcal{H}^{2}(\Sigma\cap B_{R})\geq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{0}R^{2}.

We proceed now with the proof of Lemma
2.12
.

Proof of Lemma
2.12
.

We first treat the elementary case
d=2
, and then we address the case
d=3
by using Lemma
2.14
.

Step 1
.(
d=2
)
Let
c_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}=1
and
c_{\Lambda}=(\Lambda+1)^{-1/q}\in(0,1)
. Consider
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{8\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\Omega
,
\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset
, and
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho
. Let
\bm{\gamma}
be a connected component of
\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}
intersecting
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{3\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Clearly,
\bm{\gamma}
is a regular planar curve and we have

\mathrm{diam}(\bm{\gamma})\leq\mathcal{H}^{1}(\bm{\gamma})\leq\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho.

Therefore,
\bm{\gamma}
is a closed curve inside the cube
Q_{8\rho}
. Hence, for all
0<\rho\leq c_{\Lambda}\gamma^{1/q}
, recalling that
c_{\Lambda}=(\Lambda+1)^{-1/q}
and
q\geq 1
, Lemma
A.1
yields

\displaystyle{\gamma\int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{1}\geq c^{-q}_{\Lambda}\rho^{q}\int_{\bm{\gamma}}|\kappa_{\bm{\gamma}}|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{1}\geq(\Lambda+1)\,\rho^{q}\,\big(\mathrm{diam}(\bm{\gamma})\big)^{1-q}\geq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Lambda+1)\rho^{q}\rho^{1-q}>\Lambda\rho.}

Step 2
.(
d=3
) Let
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{0}(3\sqrt{3}/8)
and
\alpha_{0}=\alpha_{0}(3\sqrt{3}/8)
be the constants in Lemma
2.14
, applied for
R=4\rho
and
\mu=3\sqrt{3}/8
. We define

(2.59)

\displaystyle c_{\Lambda}:=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\min\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big\{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}^{\frac{1-q}{q}}4\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha_{0}(\Lambda+1)^{-1/q},(4\pi)^{1/2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}^{1/q-1/2}(\Lambda+1)^{-1/q}\Big\}\,.

Consider
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{8\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\Omega
,
\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset
, and

(2.60)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{0}\rho^{2}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{0}(4\rho)^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,.

Let
K
be a connected component of
\partial E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that
K\cap Q_{3\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset
. As
\partial E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is a regular surface, we note that
K
is a regular surface as well, with
\partial K\subset\partial\Omega
. We first suppose that
K\cap\partial Q_{8\rho}\neq\emptyset
. Then, the connectedness of the regular surface
K
and the fact that
Q_{3\rho}\subset B_{3\sqrt{3}\rho/2}\subset B_{4\rho}\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{Q_{8\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
imply that

{K\cap\partial B_{3\sqrt{3}\rho/2}\neq\emptyset,\ \ \ K\cap\partial B_{4\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\emptyset\,.}

Moreover, since
\partial K\subset\partial\Omega
and
\overline{B_{4\rho}}\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{8\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\Omega
, we have that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial K\cap\overline{B_{4\rho}})=0
. Therefore, in view of (
2.60
), by applying Lemma
2.14
(or more precisely its negation) for
R=4\rho>0
,
\mu=3\sqrt{3}/8\in(0,1)
, and
\Sigma=K
, we deduce that

(2.61)

\int_{K\cap B_{4\rho}}|\bm{A}|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\geq 4\alpha_{0}\rho\,.

Using Hölder’s inequality, (
2.60
), (
2.61
), and the fact that
q\geq 2>1
, we obtain for all
0<\rho\leq c_{\Lambda}\gamma^{1/q}
,

\displaystyle\gamma\int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq c^{-q}_{\Lambda}\rho^{q}\int_{K\cap B_{4\rho}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\geq c^{-q}_{\Lambda}\rho^{q}\big(\mathcal{H}^{2}(K\cap B_{4\rho})\big)^{1-q}\Big(\int_{K\cap B_{4\rho}}|\bm{A}|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\Big)^{q}

\displaystyle\geq(c^{-q}_{\Lambda}\rho^{q})(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rho^{2})^{1-q}(4\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha_{0}\rho)^{q}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}4^{q}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha_{0}^{q}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}^{1-q}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}c^{-q}_{\Lambda}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{2}>\Lambda\rho^{2}\,,

where the last step follows from the definition of
c_{\Lambda}
in (
2.59
).

If instead we have
K\cap\partial Q_{8\rho}=\emptyset
, then
K
is closed inside the cube
Q_{8\rho}
, i.e.,
\partial(K\cap Q_{8\rho})=\emptyset
. By a classical topological-differential geometric fact regarding a lower bound on the Willmore energy of closed surfaces, we then have that

{\int_{K\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\geq 4\pi\,,}

see e.g.
[
88
, formula (0.2)]
and the references therein for its simple proof. By using again Hölder’s inequality, the fact that
q\geq 2
, and (
2.60
), as before we estimate

\displaystyle\gamma\int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq c^{-q}_{\Lambda}\rho^{q}\int_{K\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q{}_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\geq c^{-q}_{\Lambda}\rho^{q}\big(\mathcal{H}^{2}(K\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q{}_{8\rho})\big)^{1-q/2}\Big(\int_{K\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q{}_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\Big)^{q/2}

\displaystyle\geq(c^{-q}_{\Lambda}\rho^{q})(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rho^{2})^{1-q/2}(4\pi)^{q/2}\geq(4\pi)^{q/2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c{}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}^{1-q/2}c^{-q}_{\Lambda}\rho^{2}>\Lambda\rho^{2}\,,

where the last step again follows from the definition of
c_{\Lambda}
in (
2.59
). This concludes the proof.

∎

2.5.
Local thickening of sets: Proof of Lemma
2.11

This subsection is devoted to the proof of Lemma
2.11
. We start with a preliminary observation: given
\eta,\gamma>0
and
Q_{\rho}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}
for some
0<\rho\leq\eta^{7}\gamma^{1/q}
such that
\mathcal{F}_{\rm surf}^{\varphi,\gamma,q}(E\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{8\rho})\leq\Lambda\rho^{d-1}
, see (
2.37
), then by (
2.1
), Hölder’s inequality, and by
\min_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\varphi\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1
(which was assumed without loss of generality), we obtain

(2.62)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}&\leq\big(\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho})\big)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\Big(\int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|^{q}\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq\left(\Lambda\rho^{d-1}\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\left(\frac{\Lambda}{\gamma}\rho^{d-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\\
&\leq\Lambda\gamma^{-1/q}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{d-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\Lambda\eta^{7}\rho^{d-2}\,.\end{split}

Therefore, we can ensure that the
L^{1}
-norm of
|\bm{A}|
in
\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}
is small compared to
\rho^{d-2}
.

Our proof fundamentally relies on the fact that, under the above bound on the curvature,
\partial E\cap Q_{\rho}
is essentially a finite union of graphs of regular functions with suitable a priori
C^{1}
-estimates. To state this result, we introduce the following notation: given an affine subspace
L\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
of codimension
1
(i.e., a line in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
or a plane in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
), we denote by
L^{\perp}
the one-dimensional subspace spanned by a unit normal vector
\nu_{L}
to
L
. Accordingly, for
U\subset L
and
u\colon U\to L^{\perp}
, we define
{\rm graph}(u):=\{x+u(x)\colon x\in U\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We first state the result for
d=2
separately, since its proof is significantly easier than for
d=3
. Note that the parameter
\varepsilon
which appears in the next lemmata should not be confused with the one used below (
2.3
), since it serves a totally different purpose.

Lemma 2.15

(Almost straight curves)
.

There exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and an absolute constant
C_{1}\geq 1
such that for every
\Lambda>0
the following holds: for every
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, every square
Q_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\rho>0
, and every
E\in\mathcal{A}_{\rm reg}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
satisfying

\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho}\neq\emptyset,\ \mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho})\leq\Lambda\rho,\ \text{and}\ \int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{1}\leq\varepsilon\,,

there exist regular curves
(\bm{\gamma}_{i})_{i=1}^{M}
with
M\leq\Lambda
such that

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho}=\bigcup\nolimits_{i=1}^{M}\bm{\gamma}_{i}\cap Q_{3\rho}\,,

corresponding lines
L_{i}
and functions
u_{i}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{U_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to L_{i}^{\perp}
, where
U_{i}\subset L_{i}
are open segments with
\mathrm{diam}(U_{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq C_{1}\rho
, such that
{\rm graph}(u_{i})={\bm{\gamma}}_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,M
, and

\|u_{i}\|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L^{\infty}(U_{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon\rho,\quad\quad\quad\|u^{\prime}_{i}\|_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L^{\infty}(U_{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon\,.

The proof is elementary, and we refer to Appendix
A.1
. The analogous statement in dimension
d=3
is more involved: it is known as the
Approximate Graphical Decomposition Lemma
proved by
L. Simon
, see
[
88
, Lemma 2.1]
.

Lemma 2.16

(Simon’s Approximate Graphical Decomposition Lemma)
.

For any
\Lambda>0
and
\mu\in(0,1)
there exist
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon_{S}=\varepsilon_{S}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Lambda,\mu)\in(0,1)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and a constant
C_{1}=C_{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Lambda,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\geq 1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that for every
\varepsilon\in(0,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon_{S}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]
, every
\rho>0
, and every
E\in\mathcal{A}_{\rm reg}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
satisfying
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E\cap B_{\mu\rho}\neq\emptyset
, and

\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial E\cap B_{\rho})\leq\Lambda\rho^{2}\quad\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\quad\int_{\partial E\cap B_{\rho}}|\bm{A}|\,d\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq\varepsilon\rho\,,

the following holds true: there exist pairwise disjoint, closed sets
P_{1},\dots,P_{N}\subset\partial E
with

\sum\nolimits_{j=1}^{N}\mathrm{diam}(P_{j})\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\rho

and functions
u_{i}\in C^{2}(\overline{U_{i}};L_{i}^{\perp})
for
i=1,\dots,M
, with
M\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, such that

\big(\partial E\cap B_{\mu\rho}\big)\setminus\bigcup\nolimits_{j=1}^{N}P_{j}=\Big(\bigcup\nolimits_{i=1}^{M}\mathrm{graph}(u_{i})\Big)\cap B_{\mu\rho}\,.

Here, for every
i=1,\dots,M
,
L_{i}
is a two-dimensional plane in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
U_{i}\subset L_{i}
is a smooth bounded domain with

(2.63)

\mathrm{diam}(U_{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq C_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho

of the form
U_{i}=U_{i}^{0}\setminus(\bigcup_{k=1}^{R_{i}}d_{i,k})
, where
U_{i}^{0}
is a simply connected subdomain of
L_{i}
and
(d_{i,k})_{k=1}^{R_{i}}
are pairwise disjoint closed disks in
L_{i}
, which do not intersect
\partial U_{i}^{0}
.
Moreover,
\mathrm{graph}(u_{i})
is connected and
u_{i}
also satisfies the estimates

\sup\nolimits_{x\in U_{i}}|u_{i}(x)|\leq C_{1}\varepsilon^{1/6}\rho,\quad\quad\quad\sup\nolimits_{x\in U_{i}}|\nabla u_{i}(x)|\leq C_{1}\varepsilon^{1/6}\,.

Here,
\partial U_{i}^{0}
has to be understood with respect to the relative topology of
L_{i}
. Roughly speaking, the result states that, apart from sets
(P_{j})_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}j=1,\dots,N}
of small diameter, so-called
pimples
,
\partial E\cap B_{\mu\rho}
can be written as the union of finitely many graphs of regular functions with small heights and small gradients. Compare the result to the easier statement of Lemma
2.15
.

Remark 2.17

(Adaptions to the original statement)
.

We have phrased the result slightly differently compared to the original statement in
[
88
, Lemma 2.1]
, where the lemma was stated only for
\mu=1/2
but for general smooth, closed
2
-dimensional manifolds
\Sigma
. However, it is easy to verify through the proof that it is an interior
\varepsilon
-regularity
result, valid for every
\partial E\in C^{2}
and for every
\mu\in(0,1)
, up to adapting the constants. The estimate (
2.63
) is implicitly mentioned in the original statement, being a simple geometric observation, see also the proof of Lemma
2.15
in Appendix
A.1
for the analogous fact in two dimensions. Finally, the original statement has the assumption
0\in\partial E
which however can readily be generalized to requiring that
\partial E\cap B_{\mu\rho}\neq\emptyset
.

Remark 2.18

(Result on cubes)
.

As in
[
88
]
, the lemma is phrased as an interior statement for balls in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. In the application below, we will apply it on cubes, by using
Q_{8\rho}
in place of
B_{\rho}
and
Q_{3\rho}
in place of
B_{\mu\rho}
. Indeed, to this end, it suffices to note that
B_{4\rho}\subset Q_{8\rho}
and
Q_{3\rho}\subset B_{4\mu\rho}
for
\mu\in(3\sqrt{3}/8,1)
.

Both statements above involve functions which are defined with respect to suitable lines or planes, respectively. As a second preparation, we need to distinguish between
good and bad lines and planes
for a cube
Q_{\rho}
. We discuss the following definitions and properties only for planes in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
as the analogous definitions for lines in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
can be simply obtained by identifying lines in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with planes in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
with one tangent vector given by
e_{3}
.

Without restriction we suppose for the following arguments that
Q_{\rho}
is centered in
0
, as this can always be achieved by a translation.

Definition 2.19
.

Let
\theta\in(0,1/\sqrt{3})
and let
L
be a plane with normal
\nu_{L}=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3})\in\mathbb{S}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that
(L)_{3\eta\rho}\cap Q_{\rho}\neq\emptyset
, see (
1.5
). We say that
L
is a
\theta
-good plane
for
Q_{\rho}
if and only if one of the following two properties holds true:

(1)

There exist
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
,
i\neq j
, such that
|\nu_{i}|,|\nu_{j}|\geq\theta
;

(2)

There exists
k\in\{1,2,3\}
such that
|\nu_{k}|\geq\theta
and

(2.64)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}\big(L\cap Q_{3\rho},\{x_{k}=-\rho/2\}\cup\{x_{k}=\rho/2\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\geq 20\theta\rho\,.

If
L
is not a
\theta
-good plane for
Q_{\rho}
, we say that it is a
\theta
-bad plane
for
Q_{\rho}
. In the statement of Lemma
2.11
, the two different possibilities, namely
\theta
-good or
\theta
-bad planes, are reflected in the two cases described in (
2.38
)(i) and (
2.38
)(ii), respectively. The different cases of good and bad planes are depicted in Figure
4
. In the following, we denote by
\nu_{L}
a unit vector normal to
L
whose orientation will be specified in the proof below. Recall also the shorthand notation for the anistropic perimeter in (
2.36
).

L
\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap Q
\eta\rho
\eta\rho
\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap Q
L
L
\eta\rho
\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap Q

Figure 4.
Different positions of planes inside a cube. In the left and in the middle cube, the two different cases of good planes are depicted whereas the figure on the right shows a bad plane. The thick surfaces illustrate
\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap Q_{\rho}
and the dashed planes are at distance
\eta\rho
to
L
, i.e, at the maximal distance of
\mathrm{graph}(u)
from the plane
L
inside
Q_{\rho}
. In the two pictures on the left, the area of the dashed plane inside
Q_{\rho}
and of the plane
L
inside
Q_{\rho}
are comparable up to an error of order
\eta\rho^{2}
. This is the key observation for the proof of (
2.65
)–(
2.66
). In the case of a bad plane, this is in general not true.

Lemma 2.20

(Surface estimate for
\theta
-good planes)
.

There exists
\theta\in(0,1/\sqrt{3})
small enough and a constant
C_{\theta}>0
such that for any
\rho>0
and any
\theta
-good plane
L
for
Q_{\rho}
the following holds:

(i)
By letting
S_{L}:=Q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\cap(L)_{3\eta\rho}
,we obtain

(2.65)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}(\partial^{-}S_{L})\leq\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}(L\cap Q_{\rho})+C_{\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varphi_{\mathrm{max}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho^{2}\,,

where
\partial^{-}S_{L}:=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial S_{L}\setminus(-3\eta\rho\nu_{L}+L)
.

(ii)
Let
u\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in L^{\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0};L^{\perp})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for some bounded domain
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\cap Q_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset L
with
\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho
. Define
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\omega_{u}^{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=\Pi_{L}\big(\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap{Q_{\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)
, where
\Pi_{L}
denotes the orthogonal projection onto the plane
L
. Then

(2.66)

\mathcal{H}^{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\omega_{u}^{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho}))\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho^{2}\,.

Lemma 2.21

(
\theta
-bad planes)
.

There exists
\theta\in(0,1/\sqrt{3})
small enough such that for any
\rho>0
and any
\theta
-bad plane
L
for
Q_{\rho}
the following holds: let
k\in\{1,2,3\}
be the unique component such that
|\nu_{k}|\geq\theta
. Then, we have

(2.67)

\displaystyle\mathrm{either}\ -\frac{3\rho}{4}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\cdot e_{k}<-\frac{\rho}{4}\ \ \forall\,x\in L\cap Q_{3\rho},\ \ \ \mathrm{or}\ \ \ \frac{\rho}{4}<x\cdot e_{k}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{3\rho}{4}\ \ \forall\,x\in L\cap Q_{3\rho}.

The proofs of the above lemmata are elementary but tedious. They are deferred to Appendix
A.2
. We are now in the position to give the proof of Lemma
2.11
.

Proof of Lemma
2.11
.

Let
\gamma\in(0,1)
and without restriction
\Lambda\geq 1
. Consider
Q_{\rho}
centered without restriction at
0
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{12\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\Omega
and (
2.37
) holds. In the case
d=2
, we choose
\eta_{0}=\eta_{0}(\Lambda)
such that
\Lambda\eta_{0}^{7}\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, where
\varepsilon_{0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}>0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is the constant of Lemma
2.15
. Then, by (
2.37
) and (
2.62
) it is possible to apply Lemma
2.15
. In the case
d=3
, we choose
\eta_{0}=\eta_{0}(\Lambda)
such that
\Lambda\eta_{0}\leq\min\{C_{1}\varepsilon_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}S\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}^{1/6},C_{1}^{-6},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2^{-(1+q/2)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\}
, where
\varepsilon_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}S\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
C_{1}\geq 1
are the constants in Lemma
2.16
, and
c_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is the constant in Lemma
2.12
. Consequently, in view of (
2.62
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq\Lambda\eta_{0}\eta^{6}\rho^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2}\leq\left(\eta/C_{1}\right)^{6}\rho\,.

In particular, as
\eta\leq\eta_{0}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}S\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}^{1/6}
, we get
\int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}S\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rho
. This along with (
2.37
) allows to apply Lemma
2.16
in the version of Remark
2.18
. From now on, we only treat the case
d=3
since the case
d=2
is simpler (in the latter case, the sets
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{j}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}){}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}j}
below in (
2.69
) can be chosen empty). In the following,
C>0
again denotes a generic absolute constant, whose value is allowed to vary from line to line.

Step 1
.(Application of Lemma
2.16
)
By Lemma
2.16
in the version of Remark
2.18
, applied for
\varepsilon:=(\eta/C_{1})^{6}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\varepsilon_{S}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, there exist planes
L_{i}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
and functions
u_{i}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{U_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to L_{i}^{\perp}
for
i=1,\ldots,M
, with
M\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, where
U_{i}=U_{i}^{0}\setminus{\bigcup_{k=1}^{R_{i}}}d_{i,k}
for (two-dimensional) disks
(d_{i,k})_{i,k}
in the planes
L_{i}
, as well as pairwise disjoint closed subsets
(P_{j})_{j=1}^{N}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial E
such that

(2.68)

\displaystyle\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho}=\left(\bigcup\nolimits_{i=1}^{M}\mathrm{graph}(u_{i})\cup\bigcup\nolimits_{j=1}^{N}P_{j}\right)\cap Q_{3\rho}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Moreover,

(2.69)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j=1}^{N}\mathrm{diam}(P_{j})\leq C_{1}(\eta/C_{1})^{3}\rho\leq\eta\rho\,,

and for the functions
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(u_{i})_{i=1,\dots,M}
we have the
C^{1}
-estimates

(2.70)

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{x\in U_{i}}|u_{i}(x)|\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{1}(\eta/C_{1})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq 2\eta\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,,\quad\quad\quad\sup\nolimits_{x\in U_{i}}|\nabla u_{i}(x)|\leq C_{1}(\eta/C_{1})=\eta\,.

Here, in the estimates (
2.69
)–(
2.70
) we used that
\varepsilon=(\eta/C_{1})^{6}
and the fact that we can choose
\eta\leq\eta_{0}\leq C_{1}
. The nonoptimal estimate with
2\eta\rho
is introduced for later purposes in Step 4 below. To simplify the exposition, we assume for the moment that there are no pimples in
\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho}
, i.e., by (
2.68
), that we have

(2.71)

\displaystyle\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho}=\bigcup\nolimits_{i=1}^{M}\mathrm{graph}(u_{i})\cap Q_{3\rho}\,.

We defer the analysis of the case with pimples to Step 4. We fix
\theta>0
sufficiently small such that Lemmata
2.20
–
2.21
are applicable. We distinguish the two cases

(i)
L_{i}
is a
\theta
-good plane for
Q_{\rho}
,     (ii)
L_{i}
is a
\theta
-bad plane for
Q_{\rho}
.

Let us note that
I:=M\leq C_{1}
by the statement of Lemma
2.16
.

Step 2
.(Good planes)
First, let
L_{i}
be a
\theta
-good plane for
Q_{\rho}
and consider
u_{i}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{U_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset L_{i}\to L_{i}^{\perp}
. In this case, we will define
\Gamma_{i}:={\rm graph}(u_{i})\cap Q_{\rho}
and thus it is not restrictive to assume that
{\rm graph}(u_{i})
intersects
Q_{\rho}
. In the following, for notational convenience, we drop the subscript
i
and simply write
L
for the plane,
\nu_{L}
for a unit normal to
L
,
u
for the function, and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for its corresponding domain.

We will first verify that
L\cap Q_{2\rho}\subset U
. Indeed, by (
2.70
) and the fact that
{\rm graph}(u)\cap Q_{\rho}\neq\emptyset
we get that
U\cap Q_{2\rho}\neq\emptyset
for
\eta
sufficiently small. Moreover, by (
2.70
) and by taking
\eta
smaller if necessary, we get
|x+u(x)|_{\infty}<\frac{3}{2}\rho
for all
x\in L\cap Q_{2\rho}\cap\partial U
. Since
\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\partial E\cap Q_{3\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\partial Q_{3\rho}
, (
2.71
) implies that
\partial U\cap Q_{2\rho}=\emptyset
. As
U\cap Q_{2\rho}\neq\emptyset
, we conclude
U\supset L\cap Q_{2\rho}
, as desired.

We choose the following orientation for
\nu_{L}
, which is important for the definition of
\partial^{-}S_{L}
in Lemma
2.20
(i): we denote by
\bm{n}(x)
the outer unit normal to
\partial E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
at
x
and choose the orientation
\nu_{L}
as well as an orthonormal basis
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
of
L
such that the normal vector
\widetilde{\bm{n}}(x)=-(\partial_{\tau_{1}}u)\tau_{1}-(\partial_{\tau_{2}}u)\tau_{2}+\nu_{L}
to
{\rm graph}(u)
at the point
x+u(x)
satisfies
\bm{n}(x)=\widetilde{\bm{n}}(x)/|\widetilde{\bm{n}}(x)|
. Then, in view of (
2.70
), we have

(2.72)

\displaystyle\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bm{n}-\nu_{L}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|_{L^{\infty}(U)}\leq C\eta\,.

As in Lemma
2.20
, we introduce the
stripes

S_{L}:=Q_{(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho}\cap(L)_{3\eta\rho}
. We claim that

(2.73)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}\big(\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap{Q_{\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},S_{L}^{c}\big)\geq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

and

(2.74)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}\big(\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap{Q_{\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\geq\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}(L\cap Q_{\rho})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho^{2}\geq\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}(\partial^{-}S_{L})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho^{2}\,,

where
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\theta}:=C(\theta,\varphi)>0
is a constant depending only on
\theta
and
\varphi
. Here, recall the notation in (
2.36
) and the definition of
\partial^{-}S_{L}
in Lemma
2.20
(i). To obtain (
2.73
), it suffices to check that

{\rm(a)}\ \ \mathrm{dist}\Big(\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{Q_{\rho}},Q^{c}_{(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho}\Big)\geq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho\quad\text{and}\quad{\rm(b)}\ \ \mathrm{dist}\Big(\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{Q_{\rho}},(L)^{c}_{3\eta\rho}\Big)\geq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Item (a) is clear. To see (b), we first note that
\dist(L,(L)^{c}_{3\eta\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}3\eta\rho
. Then, in view of (
2.70
), for each
y\in{\rm graph}(u)\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{Q_{\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
we have
\dist(y,L)\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho
. Consequently,

{\mathrm{dist}\big(\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{Q_{\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},(L)^{c}_{3\eta\rho}\big)\geq\mathrm{dist}\big(L,(L)^{c}_{3\eta\rho}\big)-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2\eta\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\geq 3\eta\rho-2\eta\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

Regarding (
2.74
) we argue as follows: set as before
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\omega_{u}^{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\Pi_{L}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap{Q_{\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, where
\Pi_{L}
denotes the orthogonal projection onto the plane
L
. By Lemma
2.20
(ii) and the fact that
L\cap Q_{\rho}\subset{U}
, we have

(2.75)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\omega_{u}^{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho})\big)\leq C_{\theta}\eta\rho^{2}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Due to (
2.72
) and the fact that
\varphi
is Lipschitz, being a norm, we get
\|\varphi(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bm{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})-\varphi(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nu_{L}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\|_{L^{\infty}(U)}\leq C^{\prime}\eta
, for a constant
C^{\prime}
depending additionally on
\varphi
. Therefore, by (
2.75
), and the fact that we have assumed without restriction that
\min_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\varphi\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}\big(\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap{Q_{\rho}}\big)&=\int_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\omega_{u}^{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\varphi(\bm{n}(x))\sqrt{1+|\nabla u(x)|^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}(x)\geq\mathcal{H}^{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\omega_{u}^{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})(\varphi(\nu_{L})-C^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\\
&\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\geq\big(\mathcal{H}^{2}(L\cap Q_{\rho})-\mathcal{H}^{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\omega_{u}^{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho}))\big)(\varphi(\nu_{L})-C^{\prime}\eta)\\
&\geq\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}(L\cap Q_{\rho})-C_{\theta}\eta\rho^{2}\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\end{split}

where in the last step we also used the obvious bound
\mathcal{H}^{2}(L\cap Q_{\rho})\leq 3\rho^{2}
. Then, by Lemma
2.20
(i)

\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}\big(\mathrm{graph}(u)\cap Q_{\rho}\big)\geq\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}(L\cap Q_{\rho})-C_{\theta}\eta\rho^{2}\geq\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}(\partial^{-}S_{L})-C_{\theta}\eta\rho^{2}\,.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

This concludes the proof of (
2.74
).

Figure 5.
The two fundamental cases for the selection of the sets
T_{i}
(hatched). In the second figure, the dark gray set is another connected component of
\overline{S_{L}}\setminus E
that is not selected.

We now set
\Gamma_{i}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\rm graph}(u_{i})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{Q_{\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and let
T_{i}
be the connected component of
