L^{1}(\omega)
,
u_{\delta}\to u
in
L^{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and

(3.33)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\Big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{1}{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega_{h_{\delta}}^{+}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathcal{Q}(e(u_{\delta}))\,{\rm d}x+\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial\Omega_{h_{\delta}}\cap\Omega)\Big)=\mathcal{G}_{0}(u,h)\,.

Strictly speaking,
[
29
, Theorem 2.4]
only ensures that
h_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is a
C^{1}
-function, but in the proof recovery sequences are constructed for profiles of regularity
C^{\infty}
, see
[
29
, Lemma 6.4]
. Moreover, by a density argument we can assume that each
u_{\delta}
is Lipschitz on
\Omega_{h_{\delta}}^{+}
. By a diagonal argument we may further suppose without restriction that

{\|\nabla u_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{h_{\delta}}^{+})}\leq\delta^{-1/4},\quad\quad\quad\int_{\partial\Omega_{h_{\delta}}\cap\Omega}|\bm{A}_{\delta}|^{q}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma_{\delta}^{-1/2}\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

where we use that
\gamma_{\delta}^{-1/2}\to+\infty
as
\delta\to 0
. By (
3.10
), (
3.33
), the fact that
\gamma_{\delta}\to 0
, and by applying Lemma
3.12
for
\Theta_{\delta}:=\Omega^{+}_{h_{\delta}}
, we conclude that
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathcal{G}_{\delta}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}u{}_{\delta},h_{\delta})=\mathcal{G}_{0}(u,h)
. Eventually,
[
29
, Remark 6.8]
also shows that a volume constraint on the film can be taken into account, as mentioned in Remark
3.7
.

∎

We close with the short proofs of Corollaries
3.4
and
3.8
.

Proof.

In view of the above proofs, we observe that replacing
|\bm{A}_{\delta}|^{2}
by
|\bm{H}_{\delta}|^{2}
does not affect the
\Gamma
-limit, but is only relevant for the compactness results in Propositions
3.1
and
3.5
, respectively. To proceed as above, in particular in order to obtain (
3.7
)(iv) and (
3.11
)(iv), it suffices to check that, under the assumptions given in Corollaries
3.4
and
3.8
, it holds

\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\gamma_{\delta}\int_{\partial E_{\delta}\cap\Omega}|\bm{A}_{\delta}|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\gamma_{\delta}\int_{\partial E_{\delta}\cap\Omega}|\bm{H}_{\delta}|^{2}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\,.

We refer to the cases (a) and (b) discussed in Remark
2.4
.
∎

Appendix A
Some auxiliary lemmata

A.1.
Two elementary lemmata on planar curves

Lemma A.1
.

Let
q\geq 1
. For every closed, planar
C^{2}
-curve
\bm{\gamma}
it holds that

\int_{\bm{\gamma}}|\kappa_{\bm{\gamma}}|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{1}\geq(\mathrm{diam}\bm{\gamma})^{1-q}\,,

where
\kappa_{\bm{\gamma}}
denotes the curvature of the curve.

Proof.

Let
\bm{\gamma}=(\bm{\gamma}_{1},\bm{\gamma}_{2})\colon[0,L_{\bm{\gamma}}]\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{2}
be an arc-length parametrization of
\bm{\gamma}
, where
L_{\bm{\gamma}}
denotes the length of the curve. Without restriction, after a possible translation, we assume that
\bm{\gamma}(0)=\bm{\gamma}(L_{\bm{\gamma}})=0
. Let also
s_{0}\in[0,L_{\bm{\gamma}}]
be such that
|\bm{\gamma}(s_{0})|=\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bm{\gamma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|_{L^{\infty}}
. Since
|\dot{\bm{\gamma}}|\equiv 1
in this parametrization, by integration by parts and Hölder’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle L_{\bm{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{L_{\bm{\gamma}}}|\dot{\bm{\gamma}}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s=-\int_{0}^{L_{\bm{\gamma}}}\bm{\gamma}\cdot\ddot{\bm{\gamma}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{L_{\bm{\gamma}}}|\ddot{\bm{\gamma}}|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=|\bm{\gamma}(s_{0})-\bm{\gamma}(0)|\int_{0}^{L_{\bm{\gamma}}}|\kappa_{\bm{\gamma}}|\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\mathrm{diam}\bm{\gamma}\cdot L_{\bm{\gamma}}^{1-1/q}\left(\int_{0}^{L_{\bm{\gamma}}}|\kappa_{\bm{\gamma}}|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/q}\,.

This, along with the obvious fact that
L_{\bm{\gamma}}\geq\mathrm{diam}\bm{\gamma}
for every closed curve
\bm{\gamma}
, concludes the proof.
∎

We proceed with the proof of Lemma
2.15
.

Proof of Lemma
2.15
.

Clearly,
\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}
can be written as a finite union of pairwise disjoint curves
(\bm{\gamma}_{i})_{i=1}^{N}
. We denote by
(\bm{\gamma}_{i})_{i=1}^{M}
the subset of those curves intersecting
Q_{3\rho}
. It suffices to establish the desired properties for one curve only, denoted by
\bm{\gamma}
for simplicity. Additionally, we show that

(A.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{1}(\bm{\gamma}\cap Q_{8\rho})\geq\rho\,.

The latter, along with the assumption that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho})\leq\Lambda\rho
, shows that
M\leq\Lambda
.

Without restriction, we let
{\bm{\gamma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=(\bm{\gamma}_{1},\bm{\gamma}_{2})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\colon[0,L_{\bm{\gamma}}]\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{2}
be an arc-length parametrization of
\bm{\gamma}
, where
L_{\bm{\gamma}}
denotes the length of the curve. Let
L:={\bm{\gamma}}(0)+\mathbb{R}\dot{\bm{\gamma}}(0)
. Without restriction, up to an isometry we suppose that
L=\mathbb{R}(1,0)
, i.e.,
\bm{\gamma}(0)=0
,
\dot{\bm{\gamma}}(0)=(1,0)
, for notational convenience. As
\int_{\partial E\cap Q_{8\rho}}|\bm{A}|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{1}\leq\varepsilon
, we get that

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\dot{\bm{\gamma}}(s)-\dot{\bm{\gamma}}(0)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{0}^{s}\ddot{\bm{\gamma}}(t)\,{\rm d}t\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big|\leq\int_{0}^{L_{\bm{\gamma}}}|\ddot{\gamma}(t)|\,{\rm d}t\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\int_{\bm{\gamma}}|\kappa_{\bm{\gamma}}|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
for all
s\in[0,L_{\bm{\gamma}}]
.

Thus, provided that
\varepsilon_{0}
is chosen sufficiently small, we get
\dot{\bm{\gamma}}_{1}\geq 1/2
and
|\dot{\bm{\gamma}}_{2}|\leq\varepsilon
. Consequently,
\bm{\gamma}
is the graph of a regular function
u\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{U}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to L^{\perp}
for an open segment
U\subset L
containing
\bm{\gamma}(0)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
satisfying
u(\bm{\gamma}(0))=u^{\prime}(\bm{\gamma}(0))=0
, more precisely
u(x)=\bm{\gamma}_{2}(\bm{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}(x))e_{2}
. This implies
u^{\prime}=\dot{\bm{\gamma}_{2}}/\dot{\bm{\gamma}}_{1}e_{2}
and thus
\|u^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\varepsilon
.

Then, switching back to a general line
L
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, the fundamental theorem of calculus along with the fact that
u(\bm{\gamma}(0))=0
and that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(U)=\mathrm{diam}(U)\leq 8\sqrt{2}\rho
yields
\|u\|_{\infty}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|u^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{1}(U)\leq C_{1}\varepsilon\rho
. It remains to show (
A.1
). In fact, by
\gamma\cap Q_{3\rho}\neq\emptyset
and
\|u\|_{\infty}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon\rho
, provided that
\varepsilon_{0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}>0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is small enough, we have that
L\cap Q_{4\rho}\neq\emptyset
. Therefore,
\mathcal{H}^{1}(L\cap Q_{6\rho})\geq\rho
, which along with
\|u\|_{\infty}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon\rho
implies the estimate. ∎

A.2.
Lemmata on good and bad planes

In this subsection, we give the proofs of Lemmata
2.20
–
2.21
. Let
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta<1/\sqrt{3}
. Without restriction, let
Q_{\rho}
be the cube centered at
0
, and let
L
be a plane with normal
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nu_{L}:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3})\in\mathbb{S}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that
(L)_{3\eta\rho}\cap Q_{\rho}\neq\emptyset
, see (
1.5
). Before we start with the proofs, we observe the following elementary property: suppose that there exists
k\in\{1,2,3\}
such that
|\nu_{j}|\leq\theta
for both
j\neq k
. Then, we get

(A.2)

\displaystyle|(x-y)\cdot e_{k}|\leq 18\theta\rho\quad\quad\text{for all $x,y\in L\cap Q_{3\rho}$.}

Indeed, suppose without restriction (up to an appropriate reflection if necessary) that
\nu_{1}>\theta
and
|\nu_{2}|,|\nu_{3}|\leq\theta
. Thus, we have
\nu_{1}\geq\sqrt{1-2\theta^{2}}
, and an elementary computation yields

|e_{1}-\nu|^{2}\leq 2\theta^{2}+(1-\sqrt{1-2\theta^{2}})^{2}\leq 4\theta^{2}

as
0<\theta\leq 1/\sqrt{2}
. Then, there exists
R_{\nu}\in SO(3)
with
R_{\nu}\nu=e_{1}
such that
|R_{\nu}-{\rm Id}|^{2}=3|e_{1}-\nu|^{2}\leq 12\theta^{2}
, i.e.,
|R_{\nu}-{\rm Id}|\leq 2\sqrt{3}\theta
.
We fix two arbitrary points
x,y\in L\cap Q_{3\rho}
, and observe that
(x-y)\cdot\nu=0
. Therefore, we compute

\displaystyle|(x-y)\cdot e_{1}|=|(x-y)\cdot R_{\nu}\nu|\leq|(x-y)\cdot\nu|+|x-y||R_{\nu}-{\rm Id}|\leq 2\sqrt{3}\theta|x-y|\leq 18\theta\rho\,,

where in the last step we used that
x,y\in Q_{3\rho}
, and therefore
|x-y|\leq 3\sqrt{3}\rho
.

Proof of Lemma
2.20
.

The main step of the proof consists in showing the following statement:
There exists
\theta\in(0,1/\sqrt{3})
small enough and a constant
C_{\theta}>0
such that for any
Q_{\rho}
and any
\theta
-good plane
L
for
Q_{\rho}
the following holds: given a function
v\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in L^{\infty}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}V\color[rgb]{0,0,0};L^{\perp})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for some bounded domain
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L\cap Q_{\rho}\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}V\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset L
and
\|v\|_{L^{\infty}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}V\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\leq 3\eta\rho
, for all
\rho\leq r\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho
we get that

(A.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(\omega_{v}^{r}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho})\big)\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho^{2}\,,

where
\omega^{r}_{v}:=\Pi_{L}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{graph}(v)\cap{Q_{r}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\Pi_{L}
denotes the orthogonal projection onto the plane
L
.

Step 1
.(Reduction to (
A.3
))
In fact, once (
A.3
) has been shown, the statement can be derived as follows: (
2.66
) is immediate from (
A.3
). For (
2.65
), observe that
{(\partial^{-}S_{L})}_{\rm int}:=\partial^{-}S_{L}\cap{\rm int}(Q_{(1+6\eta)\rho})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
can be expressed as the graph of the constant function
z\colon L\to L^{\perp}
given by
z\equiv 3\eta\rho\nu
, i.e.,
{(\partial^{-}S_{L})}_{\rm int}={\rm graph}(z)\cap{\rm int}(Q_{(1+6\eta)\rho})=\omega^{(1+6\eta)\rho}_{z}+3\eta\rho\nu
. Then, by (
A.3
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}({(\partial^{-}S_{L})}_{\rm int})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}^{2}(\omega_{z}^{(1+6\eta)\rho})\leq\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(\omega_{z}^{(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho})\big)+\mathcal{H}^{2}(L\cap Q_{\rho})\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{2}(L\cap Q_{\rho})+C_{\theta}\eta\rho^{2}\,.

As the normal vector is constant equal to
\pm\nu
both on
{(\partial^{-}S_{L})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\rm int}
and
L\cap Q_{\rho}
, we also get

\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}({(\partial^{-}S_{L})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}_{\rm int})\leq\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\varphi}(L\cap Q_{\rho})+C_{\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varphi_{\mathrm{max}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho^{2},

where we recall the notation in (
2.36
). Consequently, to conclude the argument, we need to check that

(A.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(\partial^{-}S_{L}\setminus{(\partial^{-}S_{L})}_{\rm int}\big)\leq C_{\theta}\eta\rho^{2}\,.

Then, (
2.65
) indeed follows. To this end, note that the set
\partial^{-}S_{L}\setminus{(\partial^{-}S_{L})}_{\rm int}
consists of the six (possibly empty) sets
(L)_{3\eta\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap\partial Q_{(1+6\eta)\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap\{\pm x_{k}=\frac{1}{2}(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho\}
, for
k\in\{1,2,3\}
. We derive the estimate only for one of these sets. Without restriction let
W:=(L)_{3\eta\rho}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial Q_{(1+6\eta)\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap\{x_{3}=\frac{1}{2}(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho\}
and suppose that
W\neq\emptyset
. First, provided that
\eta_{0}
is chosen small with respect to
\theta
, we note that this set is nonempty only if (
2.64
) for
k=3
does not hold. Therefore, as
L
is a
\theta
-good plane, we necessarily have
|\nu_{1}|\geq\theta
or
|\nu_{2}|\geq\theta
and thus
|\nu_{3}|\leq\sqrt{1-\theta^{2}}
.

Let
W_{t}:=W\cap\{x\colon(x-x_{0})\cdot\nu=t\}
for some arbitrary
x_{0}\in L
. Note that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(W_{t})=0
for
|t|>3\eta\rho
and
\mathcal{H}^{1}(W_{t})\leq\sqrt{2}(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho
. Then, by the coarea formula (see
[
68
]
, formula (18.25), applied with slicing direction
\nu
in place of
e_{n}
and
e_{3}
as unit normal to the surface
W
) we get

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-(\nu\cdot e_{3})^{2}}\ \mathcal{H}^{2}(W)

\displaystyle=\int_{W}\sqrt{1-(\nu\cdot e_{3})^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(W_{t})\,{\rm d}t\leq 6\eta\rho\cdot\sqrt{2}(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho\leq C\eta\rho^{2}\,.

By using the fact that
\sqrt{1-(\nu\cdot e_{3})^{2}}\geq\theta
and by repeating the estimate for all six sets, we indeed get (
A.4
). A similar argument shows that

(A.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(L\cap(\overline{Q_{(1+12\eta)\rho}}\setminus Q_{\rho})\big)\leq C_{\theta}\eta\rho^{2}\,.

We omit the details.

Step 2
.(Proof of (
A.3
), preparations)
Let us now show (
A.3
). For convenience, we extend
v
to a function
w
defined on
L\cap\overline{Q_{(1+12\eta)\rho}}
satisfying
\|w\|_{\infty}\leq 3\eta\rho
. It suffices to show that for all
\rho\leq r\leq(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho

(A.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(\omega_{w}^{r}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho})\big)\leq C_{\theta}\eta\rho^{2},

as then the statement readily follows from the fact that

\omega_{v}^{r}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho})\subset\big(\omega_{w}^{r}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho})\big)\cup\big((L\cap\overline{Q_{(1+12\eta)\rho}})\setminus{V}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)

and that by (
A.5
) and
L\cap Q_{\rho}\subset{V}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
we have

\mathcal{H}^{2}\big((L\cap\overline{Q_{(1+12\eta)\rho}})\setminus{V}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho^{2}\,.

We start with the observation that, in view of
\|w\|_{\infty}\leq 3\eta\rho
, for all
\rho\leq r\leq(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho
it holds that

(A.7)

L\cap\overline{Q_{(1-6\eta)\rho}}\subset\overline{\omega^{r}_{w}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset L\cap\overline{Q_{(1+12\eta)\rho}}\,.

Indeed, to see the left inclusion, for each
x\in L\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{(1-6\eta)\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and every
i\in\{1,2,3\}
we estimate

\left|(x+w(x))\cdot e_{i}\right|\leq|x\cdot e_{i}|+\|w\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{(1-6\eta)\rho}{2}+3\eta\rho=\frac{\rho}{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\frac{r}{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,.

To see the right inclusion, for every
\rho\leq r\leq(1+6\eta)\rho
and
x\in\overline{\omega^{r}_{w}}
, we estimate for
i\in\{1,2,3\}

|x_{i}|\leq|(x+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x))\cdot e_{i}|+\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{r}{2}+3\eta\rho\leq\frac{(1+12\eta)\rho}{2}\,.

For notational convenience, we let
\Sigma^{r}_{w}:=\omega^{r}_{w}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho})
. We treat the two possible cases in the definition of
\theta
-good planes separately.

Step 3
.(Proof of (
A.6
), Case (1)) Let
L
be a
\theta
-good plane belonging to Case (1) in Definition
2.19
. Without restriction we suppose that
{\rm argmin}_{i=1,2,3}|\nu_{i}|=3
. This implies that
|\nu_{3}|\leq 1/\sqrt{3}
and
|\nu_{1}|,|\nu_{2}|\geq\theta
. For
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
\rho\leq r\leq(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho
, we introduce the sets

\displaystyle Q_{r}^{t}:={Q_{r}}\cap\{x_{3}=t\},\ \ \ \omega^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}:=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\overline{\omega^{r}_{w}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap\{x_{3}=t\}\ \ \mathrm{and}\ \ L^{t}:=(L\cap\overline{Q_{\rho}})\cap\{x_{3}=t\}\,.

By the fact that
|\nu_{3}|\leq 1/\sqrt{3}
, the second inclusion in (
A.7
), and by the coarea formula we have for
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho_{\eta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=(1+12\eta)\rho
that

(A.8)

\displaystyle\hskip-10.00002pt\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\mathcal{H}^{2}(\Sigma^{r}_{w})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Sigma^{r}_{w}}\hskip-1.99997pt\chi_{{Q_{\rho_{\eta}}}}\sqrt{1-(\nu\cdot e_{3})^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hskip-1.99997pt\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Sigma^{r}_{w}\cap Q_{\rho_{\eta}}^{t})\,{\rm d}t\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{-\rho_{\eta}/2}^{\rho_{\eta}/2}\hskip-1.99997pt\mathcal{H}^{1}(\omega^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}\triangle L^{t})\,{\rm d}t\,,

where we use that
\nu
is a unit normal to
\Sigma^{r}_{w}
. We now proceed with estimating
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\omega^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}\triangle L^{t})
for
|t|\leq\rho_{\eta}/2
. To this end, fixing some
z\in L
, we first introduce a parametrization of the one dimensional sets
\omega^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}
and
L^{t}
. First, for
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathbb{R}
we introduce

(A.9)

X^{t}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}):=\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0},-\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+b_{t,\nu},t\right),\ \mathrm{where}\ \ b_{t,\nu}:=\frac{z\cdot\nu-t\nu_{3}}{\nu_{2}},\,

and we observe that
L\cap\{x_{3}=t\}=\{X^{t}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\mathbb{R}\}
since
X^{t}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\cdot\nu=z\cdot\nu
. Thus, for
|t|\leq\rho/2
it holds that

L^{t}=\left\{X^{t}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in I^{t}_{L}\right\}\,,\ \text{where }I^{t}_{L}:=\left[-\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\rho}{2}\right]\cap\left[-\frac{|\nu_{2}|}{|\nu_{1}|}\frac{\rho}{2}+\frac{\nu_{2}}{\nu_{1}}b_{t,\nu},\frac{|\nu_{2}|}{|\nu_{1}|}\frac{\rho}{2}+\frac{\nu_{2}}{\nu_{1}}b_{t,\nu}\right]

and
L^{t}=\emptyset
for
|t|>\rho/2
. In a similar fashion, we obtain
\omega^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}=\emptyset
for
|t|>\rho_{\eta}/2
and for
|t|\leq\rho_{\eta}/2
we get

\omega^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}=\{X^{t}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in{I}^{r,t}_{\omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\}
, where

I^{r,t}_{\omega}:=\left\{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\,|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+w_{1}(X^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}))|\leq\frac{r}{2},\ \left|\left(-\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}+b_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\nu}\right)+w_{2}(X^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\color[rgb]{0,0,0}))\right|\leq\frac{r}{2},\ \color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left|t+w_{3}(X^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s))\right|\leq\frac{r}{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\right\}

where
w_{k}
denotes the
k
-th component of
w
. Here, we have again used the second inclusion in (
A.7
). By the area formula we get

\mathcal{H}^{1}(\omega^{r,t}\triangle L^{t})\leq\sqrt{1+\Big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{2}}\Big)^{2}}\,\mathcal{H}^{1}(I^{r,t}_{\omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangle I^{t}_{L})\text{\ \ for \ all\ }|t|\leq\rho_{\eta}/2\,.

Then, from (
A.8
) and the fact that
|\nu_{1}|\leq 1,|\nu_{2}|\geq\theta
we derive

(A.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(\Sigma^{r}_{w})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\int_{-\frac{\rho_{\eta}}{2}}^{\frac{\rho_{\eta}}{2}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\omega^{r,t}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangle L^{t})\,{\rm d}t\leq\frac{\sqrt{{3}}}{\theta}\int_{-\frac{\rho_{\eta}}{2}}^{\frac{\rho_{\eta}}{2}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(I^{r,t}_{\omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangle I^{t}_{L})\,{\rm d}t\,.

A careful inspection of the definition of
I^{r,t}_{\omega}
and
I^{t}_{L}
implies that

(A.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{1}(I^{r,t}_{\omega}\setminus I^{t}_{L})\leq\begin{cases}(r-\rho)+2\|w\|_{\infty}+\frac{|\nu_{2}|}{|\nu_{1}|}\big((r-\rho)+2\|w\|_{\infty}\big),&\text{if }|t|\leq\frac{\rho}{2}\,;\\
r+2\|w\|_{\infty},&\text{if }\text{$\frac{\rho}{2}<|t|\leq\frac{\rho_{\eta}}{2}$\,,}\end{cases}

as well as

(A.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{1}(I^{t}_{L}\setminus I^{r,t}_{\omega})\leq\begin{cases}2\|w\|_{\infty}+\frac{|\nu_{2}|}{|\nu_{1}|}2\|w\|_{\infty},&\text{if }|t|\leq\frac{\rho}{2}-\|w\|_{\infty}\,;\\
\rho,&\text{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}if }\ \color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{$\frac{\rho}{2}-\|w\|_{\infty}<|t|\leq\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}{2}$.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\end{cases}

Combining (
A.11
)–(
A.12
) and using the fact that
|\nu_{1}|\geq\theta
,
|\nu_{2}|\leq 1
, and
r\leq\rho_{\eta}
we get

\displaystyle\int_{-\frac{\rho_{\eta}}{2}}^{\frac{\rho_{\eta}}{2}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I^{r,t}_{\omega}\triangle I^{t}_{L}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq(2\rho-2\|w\|_{\infty})\Big((\rho_{\eta}-\rho)+2\|w\|_{\infty}+\frac{1}{\theta}\big((\rho_{\eta}-\rho)+2\|w\|_{\infty}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ +(\rho_{\eta}-\rho)(\rho_{\eta}+2\|w\|_{\infty})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2\|w\|_{\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho\,,

and, since
\|w\|_{\infty}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}3\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta\rho
and
\rho_{\eta}=(1+12\eta)\rho
, we conclude by recalling (
A.10
) that

\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(\omega^{r}_{w}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho})\big)=\mathcal{H}^{2}(\Sigma^{r}_{w})\leq C_{\theta}\eta\rho^{2}

for a constant
C_{\theta}>0
. This concludes the proof of (
A.6
) in Case (1).

Step 4
.(Proof of (
A.6
), Case (2))
Let now
L
be a
\theta
-good plane for
Q_{\rho}
belonging to Case (2) in Definition
2.19
, i.e., there exists
k\in\{1,2,3\}
such that
|\nu_{k}|\geq\theta
and

(A.13)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}\big(L\cap Q_{3\rho},\{x_{k}=-\rho/2\}\cup\{x_{k}=\rho/2\}\big)\geq 20\theta\rho\,.

Without restriction, we suppose that
k=3
and that
|\nu_{1}|,|\nu_{2}|<\theta
as otherwise Case (1) of the definition applies.
We start by observing that (
A.7
) yields the estimate

(A.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(\omega^{r}_{w}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho})\big)&\leq\mathcal{H}^{2}\big((L\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{(1+12\eta)\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\setminus(L\cap Q_{\rho})\big)+\mathcal{H}^{2}\big((L\cap Q_{\rho})\setminus(L\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{(1-6\eta)\rho}})\big)\\
&=\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(L\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\overline{Q_{(1+12\eta)\rho}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)-\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(L\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{(1-6\eta)\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\,.\end{split}

for
\rho\leq r\leq(1+6\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta)\rho
. In view of (
A.13
) and the fact that
\dist(L,Q_{\rho})\leq 3\eta\rho
by definition, (
A.2
) implies for
\eta_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
small with respect to
\theta
that

(A.15)

\displaystyle L\cap\big([-r/2,r/2]^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\big)\subset\overline{Q_{r}}\quad\text{for all $(1-6\eta)\rho\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r\leq 3\rho$}\,.

Let us denote by
h_{L}\colon\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
the affine function with
{\rm graph}(h_{L})=L
. Observe that
\nabla h_{L}\equiv(-\nu_{1}/\nu_{3},-\nu_{2}/\nu_{3})
and therefore

(A.16)

\displaystyle\sqrt{1+|\nabla h_{L}|^{2}}=1/|\nu_{3}|\,.

Now, by the area formula, (
A.15
), and (
A.16
) we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(L\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{(1+12\eta)\rho}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=\int_{(-\rho/2-6\eta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\ \rho/2+6\eta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{2}}\sqrt{1+|\nabla h_{L}|^{2}}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{2}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{(1+12\eta)^{2}}{|\nu_{3}|}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{2}\,,

and in a similar fashion

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(L\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{Q_{(1-6\eta)\rho}})

\displaystyle=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{(1-6\eta)^{2}}{|\nu_{3}|}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho^{2}\,.

Combining the previous two equalities with (
A.14
), we conclude

\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(\omega^{r}_{w}\triangle(L\cap Q_{\rho})\big)\leq\big((1+12\eta)^{2}\rho^{2}-(1-6\eta)^{2}\rho^{2}\big)/|\nu_{3}|\leq C_{\theta}\eta\rho^{2}

for a constant
C_{\theta}>0
depending only on
\theta
, where in the last step we used that
|\nu_{3}|\geq\sqrt{1-2\theta^{2}}
. This concludes the proof of (
A.6
).

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.21
.

Let
L
be
\theta
-bad plane for
Q_{\rho}
. Let
k\in\{1,2,3\}
be such that
|\nu_{k}|\geq\theta
and
|\nu_{j}|\leq\theta
for
j\neq k
. Since (
2.64
) does not hold, we get
\mathrm{dist}\big(L\cap Q_{3\rho},\{x_{k}=\pm\rho/2\}\big)<20\theta\rho
, where
\pm
is a placeholder for
+
or
-
. Thus, we find
x_{0}\in L\cap Q_{3\rho}
such that
|(x_{0}\pm\rho/2)\cdot e_{k}|<20\theta\rho
. This along with (
A.2
) shows that
|(x\pm\rho/2)\cdot e_{k}|<38\theta\rho
for all
x\in L\cap Q_{3\rho}
. For
\theta\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1/152
, we obtain the statement.
∎

A.3.
Rigidity estimate on cubic sets

Here, we give the proof of Proposition
2.9
. Recall the notation introduced in (
2.12
).

Proof of Proposition
2.9
.

We give the argument in detail for (
2.15
)
\rm(i)
, and only sketch the proof for (
2.15
)
\rm(ii)
, which can be derived along similar lines. For convenience, we drop the index
r
and simply write
Q
for cubes
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}
. Let us fix
Q,Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
with
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial Q\cap\partial Q^{\prime})>0
. By applying
[
50
, Theorem 3.1]
for
y
and
\mathrm{int}(Q)
or
{\rm int}({Q\cup Q^{\prime}})
, respectively, there exist
R_{Q},R_{Q,Q^{\prime}}\in SO(d)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that

(A.17)

\displaystyle\int_{Q}|\nabla y-R_{Q}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{Q}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

(A.18)

\displaystyle\int_{Q\cup Q^{\prime}}|\nabla y-{R}_{Q,Q^{\prime}}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{Q\cup Q^{\prime}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x

for an absolute constant
C>0
. Then, due to (
A.17
) and (
A.18
), we have

\displaystyle r^{d}|R_{Q}-{R}_{Q,Q^{\prime}}|^{2}=\int_{Q}|R_{Q}-{R}_{Q,Q^{\prime}}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\int_{Q}|R_{Q}-\nabla y|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{Q\cup Q^{\prime}}|{R}_{Q,Q^{\prime}}-\nabla y|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{Q\cup Q^{\prime}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

The same argument can be repeated with
Q^{\prime}
in place of
Q
for a corresponding
R_{Q^{\prime}}\in SO(d)
to obtain an estimate on
|R_{Q^{\prime}}-{R}_{Q,Q^{\prime}}|^{2}
. Then, we obtain

(A.19)

\displaystyle r^{d}|R_{Q}-R_{Q^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq C\int_{Q\cup Q^{\prime}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Based on this, we compare
R_{Q}
and
R_{Q^{\prime}}
for arbitrary
Q,Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
,
Q\neq Q^{\prime}
. We show that

(A.20)

\displaystyle r^{d}\max_{Q,Q^{\prime}}|R_{Q}-R_{Q^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq CN\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

where for notational convenience we have set
N:=\#\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
. To this end, we consider
Q,Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
,
Q\neq Q^{\prime},
and let
\{Q_{0},\ldots,Q_{M}\}\subset\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
be a simple path, i.e.,
Q_{0}=Q
,
Q_{M}=Q^{\prime}
,
Q_{i}\neq Q_{j}
for all
i\neq j
, and
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial Q_{i}\cap\partial Q_{i+1})>0
for all
i=0,\dots,M-1
. Here, we use that
(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{r}
is connected. Clearly, we have
M\leq N
. Then, due to (
A.19
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle r^{d}|R_{Q}-R_{Q^{\prime}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=r^{d}\big|\sum\nolimits_{i=0}^{M-1}(R_{Q_{i+1}}-R_{Q_{i}})\big|^{2}\leq r^{d}M\sum\nolimits_{i=0}^{M-1}|R_{Q_{i+1}}-R_{Q_{i}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CN\sum\nolimits_{i=0}^{M-1}\int_{Q_{i}\cup Q_{i+1}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

As the choice of the cubes
Q,Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U)
was arbitrary, we indeed get (
A.20
). We are now in the position to prove the statement for
R=R_{Q^{*}}\in SO(d)
for some arbitrary
Q^{*}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
. Indeed, by using (
A.17
) and (
A.20
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{r}}|\nabla y-R|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\hskip-5.69046pt\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\int_{Q}|\nabla y-R|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 2\hskip-4.26773pt\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\left(\int_{Q}|\nabla y-R_{Q}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+r^{d}\max_{Q,Q^{\prime}}|R_{Q}-R_{Q^{\prime}}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2C\sum\nolimits_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\int_{Q}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x+2Nr^{d}\max_{Q,Q^{\prime}}|R_{Q}-R_{Q^{\prime}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x+CN^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq CN^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{r}}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

In view of
N=\#\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
, this concludes the proof of (
2.15
). It remains to observe that one can choose
R={\rm Id}
if there exists
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
with
\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap\{\nabla y={\rm Id}\})\geq cr^{d}
. Indeed, by (
A.17
) one gets
\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q\cap\{\nabla y={\rm Id}\})|R_{Q}-{\rm Id}|^{2}\leq C\int_{Q}\mathrm{dist}^{2}(\nabla y,SO(d))\,\mathrm{d}x
and therefore (
A.17
) holds for
{\rm Id}
in place of
R_{Q}
, for
C
also depending on
c
. This, along with the fact that
R=R_{Q^{*}}\in SO(d)
can be chosen for an arbitrary
Q^{*}\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
, concludes the proof of (
2.15
)
\rm{(i)}
.

The proof of (
2.15
)
\rm{(ii)}
follows analogously, as a direct consequence of the following version of the Poincaré inequality on the cubic set
(U)^{r}
:

In the setting of Proposition
2.9
, there exists an absolute constant
C>0
(independent of
U
and
r
) such that for every
v\in H^{1}((U)^{r};\mathbb{R}^{d})
there exists a vector
b_{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

(A.21)

r^{-2}\int_{(U)^{r}}|v(x)-b_{v}|^{2}\leq C(\#\mathcal{Q}_{r}(U))^{2}\int_{(U)^{r}}|\nabla v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Once (
A.21
) is established, its application for
v(x):=y(x)-Rx
along with (
2.15
)
\rm{(i)}
implies (
2.15
)
\rm{(ii)}
. For the proof of (
A.21
), note that for every
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r}
, Poincaré’s inequality in
Q
gives a vector
b_{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
for which

r^{-2}\int_{Q}|v-b_{Q}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int_{Q}|\nabla v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

where
C>0
is an absolute constant. The proof can then be performed following the same steps as the proof of (
2.15
)
\rm{(i)}
above, with the obvious adaptations.
∎

A.4.
Generalized special functions of bounded deformation

Let
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open. A function
v\in L^{1}(U;\mathbb{R}^{d})
belongs to the space of
functions of bounded deformation
, denoted by
BD(U)
, if
the distribution
\mathrm{E}v:=\frac{1}{2}(\mathrm{D}v+(\mathrm{D}v)^{\mathrm{T}})
is a bounded
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\rm sym}
-valued Radon measure on
U
,
where
\mathrm{D}v=(\mathrm{D}_{1}v,\dots,\mathrm{D}_{d}v)
is the distributional differential.
For
v\in BD(U)
, the jump set
J_{v}
is countably
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-rectifiable (in the sense of
[
6
, Definition 2.57]
) and it holds that
\mathrm{E}v=\mathrm{E}^{a}v+\mathrm{E}^{c}v+\mathrm{E}^{j}v
,
where
\mathrm{E}^{a}v
is absolutely continuous with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{d}
,
\mathrm{E}^{c}v
is singular with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{d}
and such that
|\mathrm{E}^{c}v|(B)=0
if
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(B)<\infty
, while
\mathrm{E}^{j}v
is concentrated on
J_{v}
. The density of
\mathrm{E}^{a}v
with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{d}
is denoted by
e(v)
. The space
SBD(U)
is the subspace of all functions
v\in BD(U)
such that
\mathrm{E}^{c}v=0
.

We now come to the definition of the space of
generalized functions of bounded deformation

GBD(U)
and of
generalized special functions of bounded deformation

GSBD(U)\subset GBD(U)
. These spaces have been introduced and investigated in
[
31
]
. We first state the definition, see
[
31
, Definitions 4.1 and 4.2]
.

Definition A.2
.

Let
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a bounded open set, and let
v\colon U\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be measurable. We introduce the notation

{\Pi^{\xi}:=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\colon y\cdot\xi=0\},\ \ B^{\xi}_{y}:=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\colon y+t\xi\in B\}\ \ \ \text{ for any \color[rgb]{0,0,0}$B\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}$, \color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}$, $y\in\Pi^{\xi}$}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,,}

and for every
t\in B^{\xi}_{y}
we let

v^{\xi}_{y}(t):=v(y+t\xi),\qquad\widehat{v}^{\xi}_{y}(t):=v^{\xi}_{y}(t)\cdot\xi\,.

Then,
v\in GBD(U)
iff there exists a nonnegative bounded Radon measure
\lambda_{v}
on
U
such that
\widehat{v}^{\xi}_{y}\in BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(U^{\xi}_{y})
for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
y\in\Pi^{\xi}
, and for every Borel set
B\subset U

\int_{\Pi^{\xi}}\Big(\big|\mathrm{D}{\widehat{v}}_{y}^{\xi}\big|\big(B^{\xi}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{{\widehat{v}}^{\xi}_{y}}\big)+\mathcal{H}^{0}\big(B^{\xi}_{y}\cap J^{1}_{{\widehat{v}}^{\xi}_{y}}\big)\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq\lambda_{v}(B)\,,

where

J^{1}_{{\widehat{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}^{\xi}_{y}}:=\left\{t\in J_{{\widehat{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}^{\xi}_{y}}:|[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{v}}{}_{y}^{\xi}]|(t)\geq 1\right\}
.
Moreover,
v
belongs to
GSBD(U)
iff
v\in GBD(U)
and
\widehat{v}^{\xi}_{y}\in SBV_{\mathrm{loc}}(U^{\xi}_{y})
for
every

\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
y\in\Pi^{\xi}
.

Every
v\in GBD(U)
has an
approximate symmetric gradient

e(v)\in L^{1}(U;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\rm sym})
and an
approximate jump set

J_{v}
which is still
countably
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-rectifiable (cf.
[
31
, Theorem 9.1, Theorem 6.2]
). The notation for
e(v)
and
J_{v}
, which is the same as that one in the
SBD
case, is consistent: in fact, if
v
lies in
SBD(U)
, the objects coincide, up to negligible sets of points with respect to
\mathcal{L}^{d}
and
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
, respectively. The subspace
GSBD^{2}(U)
is given by

GSBD^{2}(U):=\{v\in GSBD(U)\colon e(v)\in L^{2}(U;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\rm sym}),\,\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{v})<\infty\}.

If
U
has Lipschitz boundary, for each
v\in GBD(U)
the traces on
\partial U
are well defined, see
[
31
, Theorem 5.5]
, in the sense that for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
x\in\partial U
there exists
{\rm tr}(v)(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\varepsilon^{-d}\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(U\cap B_{\varepsilon}(x)\cap\{|v-{\rm tr}(v)(x)|>\varrho\}\big)=0\quad\text{ for all $\varrho>0$}.

We close this short subsection with a compactness result in
GSBD^{2}(U)
, see
[
19
, Theorem 1.1]
.

Theorem A.3

(
GSBD^{2}
compactness)
.

Let
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be an open, bounded set, and let
(u_{n})_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n\in\mathbb{N}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\subset GSBD^{2}(U)
be a sequence satisfying

\sup\nolimits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\big(\|e(u_{n})\|_{L^{2}(U)}+\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u_{n}})\big)<+\infty.

Then, there exists a subsequence, still denoted by
(u_{n})_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n\in\mathbb{N}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, such that the set
\omega_{u}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=\{x\in U\colon\,|u_{n}(x)|\to\infty\}
has finite perimeter, and there exists
u\in GSBD^{2}(U)
with
u=0
on
\omega_{u}
such that

\displaystyle{\rm(i)}

\displaystyle\ \ u_{n}\to u\ \ \ \ \text{ in }L^{0}(U\setminus\omega_{u};\mathbb{R}^{d}),

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)}

\displaystyle\ \ e(u_{n})\rightharpoonup e(u)\ \ \ \text{ weakly in }L^{2}(U\setminus\omega_{u};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\rm sym}),

\displaystyle{\rm(iii)}

\displaystyle\ \ \liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u_{n}})\geq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cup(\partial^{*}\omega_{u}\cap U)).

In the language of
[
29
, Subsection 3.4]
, we say that
u_{n}\to u
weakly in
GSBD^{2}_{\infty}(U)
.
